## Skip intro, recap and credits (Part B)

| B1 | Duration match tolerance for database segments | Greater of 2 seconds or 0.5 percent | Stricter |
| B2 | Confidence threshold below which nothing shows | 0.7 | 0.5 or 0.85 |
| B3 | Source priority | user-marked > embedded chapters > local sidecar > online databases > on-device detection | Per-series override |

| B7 | Skip button wording | "Skip Intro", "Skip Recap", "Skip Credits" | Apple's own strings if different |
| B8 | Next episode | AVContentProposal at credits start, or at end when no credits are known | Custom |
| B9 | Settings > Playback | Skip Intro, Skip Recap, Skip Credits (Ask, Automatic, Off; default Ask); Play Next Episode Automatically (on); Detect Intros and Credits While Watching (on) | n/a |

| B11 | Fallback engine skip UI | Copies the same look and timing; recorded as HIG audit risk | n/a |

No step, plan or preparation that starts a build. `codemagic.yaml` changes only the caps: compile check 8 minutes (was 10), TestFlight 15 (unchanged). Risk noted: the compile check runs plain `swift test` of the whole package; the 100,000-item library test is now opt-in (`VP_RUN_100K=1`) so it cannot eat the budget | Decided (rule) | n/a |
| R46 | No push, no outside accounts, no contacting any provider or API (reading public documentation pages and project README/licence pages is allowed) | Decided (rule) | n/a |
| R47 | **Stray folder root cause:** PowerShell expands `$HOME` inside a double-quoted string to the Windows home-folder path (`%UserProfile%`) before bash sees it; bash strips the backslashes and creates a folder named like `CUsers<name>` or `C:Users<name>` in the current directory. Reproduced in a temp folder. Rule: never write `$HOME` or a backslash path inside a PowerShell `"..."` that goes to `wsl`/bash; use `/mnt/c/...` paths or a script file. `scripts/test-all.sh` now starts with a guard that fails if a folder named `C:*` or `CUsers*` exists in the project | Decided; guard written and run | n/a |
| R48 | R29 to R35 confirmed as recorded. Items 7 to 10 stay provisional. The 0.8 auto-accept match threshold (R37) stays PROVISIONAL and must be configurable | Confirmed | n/a |

| R50 | Remux engine: prebuilt LGPL FFmpeg for tvOS (no C compiled on Codemagic); candidates MPVKit, FFmpegKit, others; design and the parts that run in WSL (segmenter, box writer/checker, seek mapping, read-ahead scheduler, loopback server behind a protocol). Everything about AVPlayer behaviour stays an Apple TV checklist item | Research done (docs/spikes/FFMPEG_TVOS_CANDIDATES.md): recommendation **FFmpegBuild (superuser404notfound) tag 3.15.0** (LGPL-2.1+, dynamic frameworks, tvOS device and simulator slices, FFmpeg 8.1.3 plus 12 patches; young repo, 10 stars), fallback **MPVKit** (LGPL-3.0, static, bigger tree). Rejected: FFmpegKit (archived), kingslay (GPL). libdovi: prebuilt `Libdovi.xcframework` 3.3.2 (MIT) has tvOS slices. Unverified risks listed in the doc (framework MinimumOSVersion 26.0 vs binary minos 16.0, `--disable-bsfs`, dvcC/dvvC through the API, 404 MB repo clone). **PROVISIONAL** until you choose a binary | MPVKit; compile FFmpeg ourselves (rejected: no C on Codemagic) |

| R54 | Next Episode, rebuilt from your spec (docs/PLAYBACK_LOGIC.md). Interpretations where the spec is silent: countdown row choices 0, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30 s, default 10; "When Episode Ends" = Up Next only at end of file; "Ask" = Up Next at credits start with no countdown; "Off" = no Up Next, no advance, no prefetch, still marked watched; watched is marked at credits start (end of file when credits unknown) in every mode; exactly 60 s after credits counts as "not more than 60"; Skip Credits offer only in When Credits Begin and Ask and only when a next episode exists, even if the Skip Credits setting is Off; countdown starts only when playback reaches Up Next by itself (not after a seek or a resume inside credits), pausing cancels it; dismissal lasts one playback; prefetch starts 120 s before Up Next and is cancelled by Menu or leaving the player; display mode: resolution by class (SD/HD/UHD), frame rates 0.01 fps or more apart differ, HDR10+ = HDR10, all Dolby Vision profiles are one mode, an unknown format keeps the mode. The doc lists every difference from the earlier version | **PROVISIONAL** | Other readings of the same sentences |
| R55 | App-side logic (`AppLogic`, docs/APP_LOGIC.md): nine provisional interpretations (Home retention and row sizes, sort defaults, the Play / Resume / Play Again rule, Now Playing layout, delay step size, the Up Next countdown handling) are listed in that doc. **Mismatch recorded:** AVKit presents a proposal at `contentTimeForTransition` and counts its own automatic acceptance from the end of playback, while our Up Next starts at the credits start with its own countdown, so the tvOS wrapper turns Apple's countdown off and sets the transition time to the current playhead; whether AVKit then shows the proposal at once is **unverified** (Apple TV checklist). `LibraryStore` has no "list series" call yet (the Shows screen reads every episode page); to add in `LibraryIndex` | **PROVISIONAL** | Other readings; a different proposal mechanism |

## Decisions, round 5 (storage model, platform audit, native tvOS 27 standard) — all PROVISIONAL
| # | Decision | Status | Alternatives |
|---|----------|--------|--------------|
| R56 | **Storage model.** Apple's tvOS guide says an app can access only 500 KB of persistent local storage (`NSUserDefaults`), all other data must be purgeable by the system, the cache directory can be deleted when space is low and the app is not running, and user data should be stored in iCloud (verified from the page itself, quotes in docs/STORAGE_MODEL.md). So: user data goes to CloudKit (private database) and the iCloud-synced Keychain, a small essentials copy (under 200 KB, test-enforced) in `UserDefaults`, and everything rebuildable (SQLite index, metadata, artwork, subtitles) lives in Caches and is purgeable. The SQLite index is no longer the home of user data. Work in progress | **PROVISIONAL** (changes LibraryIndex and R34) | A store the user controls (export/import only); document-based storage on a source |
| R57 | **"Offline downloads" cannot be persistent on tvOS.** Reclassified to "prefetch to cache, best effort, never promised" | **PROVISIONAL** (spec change, to be written in docs/FEATURE_BACKLOG.md and SPECS.md, recorded here so it is not silent) | Drop the feature |
| R58 | iCloud container `iCloud.com.liamp.videoplayer`; CloudKit capability, Keychain sharing in `project.yml`; the steps you must do in your Apple Developer account later go in docs/CODEMAGIC_SETUP.md. **Nothing is registered** | **PROVISIONAL** | A different container name |
| R59 | **Platform audit** (docs/PLATFORM_AUDIT.md): a table of every tvOS capability that matters to a video player, with Apple links and a decision per row; rows with no decision are called out as "needs the user's decision" with a recommendation. The cut list stays cut | Work queued | n/a |
| R60 | **Native standard: tvOS 27 and Liquid Glass.** Every screen is built from system components, system text styles, semantic colours, SF Symbols only, system focus and system materials; no custom or branded look; no Apple names or product text in the app UI; docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md holds the native component map; `scripts/design-lint.sh` enforces it. Where a hard rule conflicts with a verified API limit, the conflict is recorded as a row, not silently bent | **PROVISIONAL** | Custom-styled UI (rejected by you) |
| R61 | **Deployment target tvOS 27.0** and Xcode 27.x / Swift 6.4 (changes provisional item 9 "tvOS 18"). Codemagic image changes to the newest Xcode 27 image Codemagic documents; nothing else in codemagic.yaml changes and no build starts. The Codemagic machine is a Mac mini M4, which changes the compile-minute estimate (unmeasured) | **PROVISIONAL** | tvOS 18 with availability checks (recorded) |
| R62 | tvOS 27 release-note items to adopt: buttons no longer take their tint from the asset-catalog accent colour; new `@State` macro rules; `.bordered` text field style; `AsyncImage` HTTP caching (decide whether it replaces our artwork cache; cached data is purgeable); the scene-based life cycle is required or the app fails to launch (check `App/VideoPlayerApp.swift`); Background Assets replaces On Demand Resources | Work queued | n/a |
| R64 | **Credentials do not sync on tvOS.** Apple's `kSecAttrSynchronizable` page says Keychain items on tvOS never leave the device where they are created (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/ksecattrsynchronizable), so "credentials in the iCloud Keychain" cannot work. Not built: option B, secrets in CloudKit `encryptedValues`. | R71 | **Decision D-KC1, passwords on tvOS (PROVISIONAL option B).** Server passwords are kept in the local Keychain on the device AND mirrored into CloudKit encrypted fields (`CKRecord.encryptedValues`) in the user's private database; a reset device restores them from the cloud copy. Checked on the page: `encryptedValues` is available on **tvOS 15.0+** (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/cloudkit/ckrecord/encryptedvalues), values are encrypted on the device before upload, only NEW fields may be encrypted, it is not allowed in the public database, and encrypted fields cannot be used in query predicates or sorts (the code fetches by record name, not by query). When the user resets their encrypted data key CloudKit removes the zone (`CKErrorUserDidResetEncryptedDataKey`, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/cloudkit/ckerroruserdidresetencrypteddatakey): the code treats that as `recoveredAfterKeyReset` and uploads the local copies again. The 'tvOS Keychain does not sync through iCloud' claim comes from the earlier storage-model research and was not re-fetched on a page today. Settings > Storage and Cloud says where passwords are kept and what a reset does (`CredentialStorageText`, tested). Never compiled or run against CloudKit | **PROVISIONAL** | Option A (fallback): Keychain only, no cloud copy; after a device reset every password is entered again. Warnings: 179 warning lines with a file position, 72 distinct (the rest repeat per architecture); the log has about 500 lines containing "warning:" because each is echoed with its source excerpt. Of the 72: 70 are in the vendored third-party code (Citadel 40, AMSMB2 25 C integer-precision notes, swift-nio-ssh 5: Swift 6 Sendable notices, three unused throwing Tasks in Citadel ExecHandler, two "will be an error in a future language mode" default-expression notes in Citadel RemotePortForward; left alone, it is upstream code), 1 in App (PlayerCoordinator conforms to AVPlayerViewControllerDelegate across the main actor: an error only in Swift 6 mode, backlog 35) and 1 in PlaybackLogic (NextEpisode.swift:438 `var out` never mutated, reported once per architecture: harmless, fixed). Fixes in the commit after 5703a92: (1) `makeSource`: the sidecar reader's closure is now spelled `@Sendable … async` and calls the main-actor `SourceFactory` with `await` (the factory reads main-actor state, so it is not made nonisolated); (2) `setRecorder`: `SQLiteLibraryStore.init(path:recorder:)` takes the recorder (default nil), and AppServices builds the ledger first and opens the store with it, so the recorder is in place before the first change can happen (setRecorder had no other caller in App/; the tests still use it); (3) `UDSyncReport` has an explicit public init with the same defaults. New tests: Tests/LibraryIndexTests/FirstCompileFixTests.swift (3; UserData imported without @testable, so they compile only if the API is public). Also: `Conflicting identity for swift-nio-ssh` fixed (Vendor/Citadel/Package.swift uses `../swift-nio-ssh` by path; scripts/setup-citadel.sh makes and checks that change); NextEpisode `var` → `let` | **Done (3 errors fixed; the rest of the app is still unproven until the next compile)** | |

| R163 | **iCloud backup of generated data (not the media), PROVISIONAL** (docs/BACKUP.md). The library index (`library.sqlite`, copied with SQLite's backup API), the online-metadata answers, `matches.json` and the pictures read from sources go to the user's PRIVATE CloudKit database, zone `LibraryBackup`: one `BKManifest` record per set (`library`, `artwork`; the manifest JSON is a CKAsset) and one `BKChunk` record per 1 MiB piece, named by the SHA-256 of its bytes (content-addressed: incremental upload, dedup, immutable pieces). Versioning: manifest `format` (1) and `librarySchema`; a newer one is never restored or overwritten; `generation` +1 only after every piece is uploaded, so a restore never mixes versions; the previous generation is kept. Merge rule: the database is restored only into an empty library or when the user confirms; other files: newer wins; watch state and settings come back through the existing user-data sync (newest edit wins). Switches: library data (on), artwork (on; option "only artwork for items in your library"), settings and watch state (on; already synced by UserData). Never: the media, passwords, tokens, API keys (the engine refuses a file that contains one). Runs debounced (2 min) after library changes, at launch when the last backup is over a day old, and on demand; never through background sync (D8). Not backed up: `Caches/remote-artwork` (URL cache, refetched). Alternatives: (a) NSPersistentCloudKitContainer / Core Data mirroring (rejected by the brief; thousands of records for regenerable data); (b) one CKAsset per file without chunking (simpler, but every change re-uploads the whole 10–100 MB index); (c) CKSyncEngine for the chunks (more code for no gain: pieces are immutable, nothing to merge); (d) iCloud Drive / ubiquity container files (NOT CHECKED whether tvOS offers it; the files would be visible and deletable in Files on other devices); (e) no backup, rely on rescans (status quo). The CloudKit adapter is UNVERIFIED until a CI compile and a device run. | PROVISIONAL |
| R164 | **Launch safety and ad-hoc signing of the screenshots job, PROVISIONAL** (docs/API_USAGE.md "Launch safety"). Fourth CI run: every UI test reported the app as crashed and every screenshot showed the system Home Screen. Most likely cause (not proven): `CKContainer(identifier:)` created in three adapter initializers during `AppServices.init`, in an unsigned build that has no iCloud entitlement. Fix: the container is made lazily, once, inside an Objective-C `@try`; a failure becomes "iCloud is not available in this copy of the app" in Settings and the sync line; UI-test modes use in-memory fakes; the App Group folder is checked before use. **Signing decision:** the screenshots job (job 4) builds with ad-hoc signing (`CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=-`, no team, no profile, no certificate, no secret) so the simulator build carries its entitlements as a device build would; if that build fails it falls back to unsigned with a warning. The device build (job 2) stays unsigned. Not verified on an Apple page: that the simulator applies ad-hoc-embedded entitlements. Alternative: keep every job unsigned and rely on the code guards alone (the app must then launch with iCloud reported as unavailable). A LAUNCH-SMOKE step (scripts/ci-launch-smoke.sh) launches the app plain and with `-UITestEmpty` before the UI tests and prints LAUNCH OK or the crash report. Not fixed until a CI run shows LAUNCH OK. | PROVISIONAL |
| R165 | **Short texts never cut and never wrapped (rule S1, owner requirement), PROVISIONAL.** Card titles, subtitles, badges, row titles and detail facts are drawn on one line (`lineLimit(1)`) and may shrink to fit (`minimumScaleFactor`); if they still do not fit, whole segments are left out, lowest priority first (`ShortLine` in AppLogic, `FittingLine` / `FittingText` in App/Shared), never half a word and never "…". Priorities: episode code > episode title > time left; movie: time left > year; badges keep their order and drop from the end ("4K · Dolby Vision · Dolby Atmos" → "4K · Dolby Vision" → "4K"; the real names are never shortened). **Conflict 1 (scale):** the owner asked for a minimum scale of about 0.7; Apple's tvOS minimum text size is 23 pt (Typography, Accessibility pages). Provisional: the larger of the two wins, `max(0.7, 23 / style size)`, so Caption 2 (23 pt) never shrinks and loses segments instead; Body may shrink to 0.79; Title 1 to 0.7. **Conflict 2 (wrapping):** Typography says that at larger text sizes labels should use more lines rather than truncate; S1 says never wrap. Provisional: S1 (one line) for short texts, because nothing is cut either way; longer descriptive sentences (Discover "where to watch", overviews, honesty lines) keep wrapping. Alternatives: (a) Apple only: `lineLimit(2)` with truncation at the end; (b) 0.7 exactly, below 23 pt for Caption/Caption 2; (c) wrap only when Larger Text is on. `ViewThatFits` was not used: it chooses by ideal size and ignores `minimumScaleFactor`, so it would drop segments that could have fitted by shrinking. Fixed in code; verify in next screenshots. | PROVISIONAL |

| R167 | **Tab bar too wide for seven tabs (screenshot review item 1). PROVISIONAL decision: option (c), Discover folded into Search; owner to confirm.** The bar holds Home, Search, Movies, Shows, Files, Discover, Settings. Apple, Tab bars (tvOS): when there are more tabs than fit, the system fades the rightmost one at the edge, and when the bar scrolls it fades the left edge too; there is no "More" tab on tvOS. Our RootView uses the system TabView with no custom bar, tint or style, so the clipped Settings tab and the first tab scrolling off on the Settings screen are the system's own overflow, and the only fix is fewer tabs. Options: (a) **keep seven tabs** (not chosen, can be changed) — pro: nothing moves; con: the last tab is always faded and the bar scrolls; (b) **Settings as an icon-only tab** (not chosen, can be changed) (gear symbol, label kept for VoiceOver) — pro: small change, Settings stays one press away; con: Apple's Tab bars page asks for short text labels, unproven that it fits; (c) **merge Discover into Home or Search** (a row or a scope) (**CHOSEN, PROVISIONAL**: into Search) — pro: six text tabs very likely fit, and Discover already needs a TMDB key to show anything; con: Discover loses its own place; (d) **merge Movies and Shows into one "Library" tab** (not chosen, can be changed) with a picker — pro: five or six tabs; con: one more press to reach either, a new screen to design (new feature); (e) **move Settings out of the bar** (not chosen, can be changed) (e.g. a button on Files or Home) — pro: frees a tab; con: harder to find, a custom entry point. (f) **sidebar instead of a top tab bar** (not chosen, can be changed) (`tabViewStyle(.sidebarAdaptable)`, tvOS 18+; Apple: "macOS and tvOS always show a sidebar", https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/tabviewstyle/sidebaradaptable) — pro: an Apple style, every tab keeps its label, no overflow; con: a different look for the whole app, the sidebar takes width from the content, never seen in this app. Recommendation, not built: (c), then (b) if it still does not fit. **Owner, 2026-11-18: leave the bar exactly as it is for now; decision later.** **Owner, 2026-11-18 (later), PROVISIONAL: option (c), Discover into the Search tab.** Built: six tabs Home, Movies, Shows, Files, Search, Settings (AppLogic `AppTabID`); Search keeps the system `.searchable` field and keyboard at the top and shows Discover below it while the field is empty, library results once something is typed, never both (AppLogic `SearchTabContent`, 8 tests); clearing the field brings Discover back without reloading it; Show Discover off leaves the plain "Search Your Library" prompt and sends nothing; the "Discover Needs a Key" wording is unchanged; Settings > Home Screen and Online Lookup keep every Discover option. Discover has no detail pages (a poster toggles the watchlist), so there is no Discover page to go Back from. Unverified: where tvOS draws a `Tab(role: .search)` (Apple's pages do not say; the earlier review saw it second, after Home); the screenshot job's "02-home-empty-TABBAR" note lists every tab's frame and says "TAB BAR OK" or "TAB BAR PROBLEM". Fixed in code, verify in next screenshots (02, 06, 75, 76). To undo: put the Discover `Tab` back in RootView and `discover` back in `AppTabID`. **Evidence for alternatives (d) merge Movies and Shows and (e) move Settings out of the tab bar (#17 B, 2026-11-18), PROVISIONAL: keep the six system tabs.** The Larger Text screenshots 80, 81, 83 and 84 (from the #17 review) show the label "Settings" cut to "Sett" with an edge fade; the icons grow but the labels do not. In 82, with Settings focused, the bar has scrolled and "Home" is cut at the left edge. The cause in those pictures was the app's own override reaching the tab bar, which R175 fixed. Apple's HIG describes this exact tvOS behaviour: "If there are more items than can fit in the tab bar, the system truncates the rightmost item by applying a fade effect that begins at the right side of the tab bar. If there are enough items to cause scrolling, the system also applies a truncating fade effect that starts from the left side" (https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/tab-bars, tvOS). So the clipping is recorded as system behaviour. That the bar scrolls is what keeps every tab reachable; the HIG implies it but does not say it in those words. No custom tab bar. Checked in CI: `tabBarCheck` writes each tab's frame ("TAB BAR OK" or "TAB BAR PROBLEM") on 02 and on the Larger Text Home shot 80. Still needed, on a device: with the real Text Size at its largest (V-15), move along the tab bar from Home to Settings and back, and write down whether each of the six tabs can be focused and opened and what each label shows. If a tab cannot be reached, choose (d) or (e) then. **Owner, 21 Nov 2026 (scan of ec27d52, finding 3): the sidebar, option (f); built as R359.** The six tabs of (c) are kept as the sidebar's items. | Open (owner to confirm) |

Apple's page for `navigationDestination(for:destination:)` says to add it to a view inside a NavigationStack, so a poster, a show card, a Files folder and the Top Shelf link most likely pushed nothing. Fixed: `.routeDestinations()` is now on each tab's root view inside its stack. Not proven until the next CI screenshots show the show page, the movie page and a Files folder. | Fixed in code |
| R169 | **Launch time-box for iCloud, PROVISIONAL.** Before: the launch scan ran only after the iCloud account check, the restore of an empty library and the whole start-up sync had finished, so a slow or offline iCloud held the scan back without limit (the screens themselves already showed the library from the local database). Now (`AppServices.launch`, `TimeBox` in AppLogic): the account check and the iCloud start-up run in the background; the launch scan waits for them at most **8 s** (`TimeBox.launchWait`) and then starts, while the iCloud work finishes on its own (not cancelled). A scan that is asked for while one runs now waits for it (the start-up rebuild of a lost database needs the finished index). **Restore rule:** the restore of an EMPTY library and the scan would both write the library database, so (a) if the restore is already writing it when the box runs out, the scan waits for the database part only (not the pictures, not the sync; the restore banner is on screen); (b) if the restore has not started yet, it is not started at all on this launch (Settings > Restore from iCloud still works) and the scan builds the library from the sources. Remaining risk: in case (a) a restore that hangs holds the scan back (the library is empty and the banner shows meanwhile). Alternatives: a longer box (15–30 s, a slower first launch on a new Apple TV); never restore automatically (always rescan); restore and scan into separate databases and merge (new work). Unit-tested with a fake clock in WSL; unproven on a device with a slow or offline iCloud (docs/COVERAGE.md). | PROVISIONAL |
| R170 | **Accessibility-setting screenshots (rule 17), PROVISIONAL.** Larger Text (`accessibility3`) and Bold Text are drawn by the app itself in DEBUG UI-test mode (`-UITestTextSize`, `-UITestBoldText`) through `dynamicTypeSize` and `legibilityWeight`, the two values SwiftUI documents as settable: the app's own views follow them, the system tab bar does not (it keeps the simulator's setting). Increase Contrast, Reduce Transparency and Reduce Motion are read-only in SwiftUI, so the CI job turns them on in the simulator with `xcrun simctl ui`, choosing the option by reading `xcrun simctl help ui` on the runner (scripts/ci-a11y-setting.sh); a setting simctl does not offer is logged NOT APPLIED and gets no screenshots. Each step waits at most 10 s. Not proven until a run: which simctl options exist in Xcode 27.2, whether the app picks them up without a reboot, that the sample library loads within 10 s, and the extra job time (5 settings × 5 screens). Reduce Motion cannot be judged from still pictures. Alternatives: set the system settings in the simulator's preferences files (undocumented); test on a device only. | PROVISIONAL |

"Sample Remake (2005)" and "Sample Remake (2019)" are two shows; one show when at most one year is known or the years match. I agree with the rule: a remake or reboot merged into the original mixes two sets of episodes with the same numbers, which is worse than a rare wrong split. Built as a pass after each scan (schema 4 `files.series_year`, `MediaSorter.revision` 2), so a library without such a pair keeps every key and watch state. A file with no year beside two years: since #19 C (PROVISIONAL) it goes to Needs Review with the note "Two shows share this name …", so the user decides; it goes back by itself when one year is left, and a Move or online-check result is kept. Alternatives: (a) always put the year in the show key when the name has one, simpler, but "Show (2019)" and "Show" with no year would then be two shows and every existing yeared show would get a new key and lose its watch state (watch state is stored by item key); (b) keep merging (the earlier behaviour). Unproven: on-screen look of split shows; device timing. |

Now the override is applied to each tab's content and the player (RootView), so the tab bar keeps the simulator's own size, as on a device it keeps the device's (the code comment already said so; the code did not). Unproven: that sheets opened inside a tab (Add a Source, Fix Match, file details) inherit the override (unverified, from memory; the next accessibility screenshots show it); what the SYSTEM tab bar does on a device with Larger Text at the largest size (DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST_VISUAL.md V-15). The "another language" case is covered by unit tests only, since local files carry no language. *Unproven:* ImageIO decoding and the drawn size on a device (from Apple's ImageIO pages, compiled only in CI); how a logo looks with Larger Text (it does not scale with text: a picture). | R196 | **libdovi references `posix_spawnp` (#23 C), OPEN.** Checked locally with llvm-nm on the tvOS device slices: `DolbyVisionRPULib.xcframework/tvos-arm64_arm64e/libdovi.a` has an undefined `_posix_spawnp` in one Rust standard-library object (`std-….std….cgu.08.rcgu.o`, once per arm64/arm64e slice; the simulator slice has it too). libnfs.a and the five codec frameworks reference no process-spawning function (posix_spawn, posix_spawnp, fork, vfork, exec*, system). Whether the app binary imports it is **unknown**: the linker takes that object only if the app needs a symbol in it, and the main binary has never been linked (the first CI compile failed). The device-build inspection now reports it (process-spawning.txt). Whether tvOS allows it: unverified, from memory, the tvOS SDK headers mark `posix_spawn`/`posix_spawnp`, `fork` and `system` unavailable on tvOS; the symbol still resolves at link time (the package tests that link libdovi passed on the tvOS 27.2 Simulator), and calling it at run time would fail. libdovi never starts a process itself; the reference comes from Rust's `std::process`. Options, **not chosen**: (a) do nothing if the next device build shows the app binary does not import it; (b) rebuild libdovi so the reference is gone (for example build without the code path that uses `std::process`, or with `-Z build-std` and dead-code stripping), which needs a Rust toolchain on a Mac; (c) provide a stub `posix_spawnp` that returns an error, linked into the app, so the import resolves to app code instead of libSystem. Decide after the next device build's process-spawning.txt. | OPEN | (a), (b) or (c) |

Fixed for that one spot only: the wide card is now Apple's tvOS content lockup, as the episode card already is (#30): one `.borderless` NavigationLink whose label holds the artwork and the title ("Creating a tvOS media catalog app in SwiftUI", https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/creating-a-tvos-media-catalog-app-in-swiftui; `borderless` tvOS 17+); the second line and the badge line stay below, outside. Proven here: the structure (`testTheContinueWatchingCaptionIsInsideTheFocusedButton`, ScreenChecksTests). Only CI can prove the motion: 50-home-continue-watching-caption and -later (test04, Sample Movie 7 focused 3 s, `mustChangeAfter`). Unverified: that the system lifts the artwork (a custom view, not an `Image`) and that the card's look beside the `.card` posters is acceptable. Not changed (listed only): portrait cards (`PosterCard`), `SeriesCard`, `CardRow` titles keep the title outside the button with no marquee. **#36 group 1 d and group 4 (20 Nov 2026):** for the lockup cards (Continue Watching, episodes) the marquee is withdrawn: the third run showed 50 / 50-later and 56 / 56-later identical with the caption cut mid-word ("… that i"), so the claim that the system scrolls a focused long caption is removed from the notes and comments; R264 replaces it for those captions (shrink to the floor, then wrap). Rows that use `fadesAtRest` (the Files rows) are unchanged. | PROVISIONAL. | R231 | **The show page froze test04 (#31 item 1): cause found by reading the code, fix PROVISIONAL, unproven until CI.** Run of 20 Nov on c8716df: after 54-shows the relaunch on Sample Show 1 drew the page, then nothing changed for 30 s ("main thread busy for 30.0s", UITests/ScreenshotTests.swift:50). The lookup path is not it: with `-UITestFixtures` `OnlineServices.details` returns the fixture details before any layer is built (no key, Free Data Sources off, #29 not reached), and `keyRevision` changes only on `rebuild()` (launch, Settings). Cause: `FittingText` (App/Shared/FittingLine.swift, R199 since #25 B4) measured the width of its OWN scaled text and, when the text did not fit at the readable minimum, set its scale to 0.98 of that width; the smaller text measured smaller, so each layout pass started another (a state change that re-triggers itself) on the episode captions ("S2 E1 · Title", containerRelativeFrame cards) of the show page. The same loop explains the unreadable "S2 E3" caption in 77. It depends on timing (which captions are laid out first), which fits test04 failing while test13 passed the same page. Fix: below the readable minimum the text takes the whole offered width (`frame(maxWidth: .infinity)`), so the measured width no longer depends on the scale, and a width is written only when it changed by half a point or more. Test: `test15_ShowPageResponsive` opens the page, moves the focus, and fails when any query waits more than 5 s for the main thread (note "show-page-responsive"); it runs before test04 in the part-2 job. Not proven: that this loop is the only cause (Instruments or a sample of the main thread in CI would prove it), and that the episode captions look right after the change. **#33 F:** the run of 20 Nov hung again in part 1 (test12, test13) while test15 and test04 passed on the same page in part 2: see R242. Not fixed. | PROVISIONAL. Alternatives: (a) a custom `Layout` that reads the proposed width; (b) drop the below-minimum scaling (R229 floor) so no width feeds back at all |

Free standard runners only. Options: (a) view controls (Sort, Filter, Saved Views) leading, Needs Review and Jump to Letter as a second group, the count as trailing text; (b) the same with Sort, Filter and Saved Views as symbols only; (c) keep one undivided row. **Pick (b)**, from the HIG Toolbars page (https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/toolbars, read in #32; "No additional considerations for tvOS"): "Group toolbar items logically by function and frequency of use"; "aim for a maximum of three" groups; "Prefer simple, recognizable symbols for items instead of text, except for actions like edit that aren't well-represented by symbols"; "Keep actions with text labels separate" (side by side they "can create the illusion of a single action"). So the three view controls are one group of symbols, the two text-labelled tools a second group, the count plain text at the trailing edge. Code: App/Shared/GridToolbar.swift (`ToolbarSymbol`, `.labelStyle(.iconOnly)`; the name stays the accessibility label). The gap between groups is the row spacing plus the system padding (no number). Kept: one line at every size with the More menu (R200), the 80 pt side margin, the 320 pt grid, the 40/100 pt spacing, all control IDs. **Risks, unverified until the next run:** symbol-only controls show no name on screen (tvOS has no pointer hover; HIG Buttons: "Consider using text when a short label communicates more clearly than an icon"); the bookmark symbol for Saved Views is the least self-explaining; whether the group gap is visibly wider (UI test `checkToolbarGroups` measures it). "Jump to Letter" keeps the SF Symbol `textformat.abc` (`Image(systemName:)`); its baseline offset in 07 was not reproduced here (no picture available locally): unverified, the new 07-*-toolbar pictures show it. **#33 G (20 Nov 2026):** the run measured 60 pt inside a group and 76 pt between groups (16-* -TOOLBAR notes), so the groups did not read as groups. Risk: the wider row may send a control into More at Larger Text (R200). **#34 3 (20 Nov 2026):** computed from the run's notes (07-*-larger-text-GROUPS) and the count measured on the pictures: at Larger Text the controls take 1,270 pt (125 + 114 + 107; 476 + 448), the count "7 movies" 179 pt; the space between the 80 pt margins is 1,760. #33's 60 inside a group and 140 between need 1,769 pt (Movies) plus the system's unknown space before the count (0 to 60 pt): the row would fold into More. With 60 inside, 120 between fits only if that space is under 12 pt (unproven). **Now, PROVISIONAL:** explicit spacings in the toolbar only (`GridToolbarLayout`: 48 inside a group, 100 between, 40 before the count; `ActionRow(spacing:)`, nil everywhere else): Larger Text needs 1,733 (Movies) and 1,721 (Shows) of 1,760; the default size 1,345 and 1,336; ratio 2.08 at both sizes. Proven on the notes by ScreenChecksTests `testTheToolbarRowFitsAtLargerTextWithTheseGaps`, which reads the constants from GridToolbar.swift. test16 no longer opens a control through More, and the groups check fails when the row folded into More. | R240 | **Text contrast target (#33 K), PROVISIONAL target 4.5:1.** HIG Accessibility (developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/accessibility, read 20 Nov 2026): Accessibility Inspector uses WCAG AA, 4.5:1 for text up to 17 pt, 3:1 at 18 pt and for bold text; no additional considerations for tvOS; prefer system colours (they adapt to Increase Contrast). tvOS text is at least 23 pt (same page), so that table alone gives 3:1; 4.5:1 is our stricter choice. Measured on the run of 20 Nov (ScreenChecks `Contrast`: background = the most frequent colour of the text's frame, text = the median of the 3 % of pixels farthest from it in luminance; limits: anti-aliasing, translucent material behind the text, frames much wider than the text): 31 footer 2.77:1, 31 summary 4.35:1, 78 Media Info labels 3.56:1 and 4.24:1. Changed ONLY: the Settings summary and Form text (`SettingsPane`) and the Media Info labels, to the primary system colour. The UI tests write a "-CONTRAST" note at 31 and 78 and never fail on it. No system secondary text here passes, so none is kept. A lighter system secondary (`.secondary` under Increase Contrast) cannot be measured on these pictures. **Pick: primary at 70 %** (`ReadableSecondary`, App/Shared/LayoutParts.swift), the lowest opacity that reaches 4.5:1 on every background (lowest 5.28:1; 60 % fails at 4.34:1), so it keeps the most distance below primary. Applied ONLY to the Settings summary, the Form's secondary level (`foregroundStyle(_:_:)`, tvOS 15: "Sets the primary and secondary levels of the foreground style in the child view"; the section footers are expected to use it, unverified) and the Media Info labels; #33 K's full primary is replaced. Proven on Linux: `testTheReadableSecondaryOpacityPassesOnTheRunsBackgrounds` (reads the constant). CI only: the real colours (next 31 and 78 -CONTRAST notes). Side effect to watch: `foregroundStyle(_:_:)` turns symbol images in the Form to the palette rendering mode (Apple's page). HIG Accessibility on Increase Contrast: "If your app doesn't provide this minimum contrast by default, ensure it at least provides a higher contrast color scheme when the system setting Increase Contrast is turned on" (https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/accessibility); the HIG gives no tvOS-specific contrast rule. Same pattern NOT changed (listed only): `TaskPageHeader` summary (SettingsPane.swift), the facts line and other .secondary text of the detail page, card second lines, the grid count, Files summaries (PaneColumns default). | PROVISIONAL. Alternatives: (a) 3:1 as in the HIG table, no change; (b) keep `.secondary` and rely on Increase Contrast; (c) a heavier weight for labels; (d) primary colour with a smaller size for the hierarchy; #34 5b: (e) full primary (#33 K, 8.77 to 14.16:1, no hierarchy by colour); (f) primary at 80 % (6.33:1 lowest, more margin, less hierarchy); (g) primary at 60 % (fails 78 Resolution at 4.34:1) |

The hang capture (`sample`, notes/hang-sample-<test>.txt) and the 5 s responsiveness checks stay: if test12 or test13 hang again on a clean simulator, part 1's state is ruled out and the capture is the evidence. | Open (cause). | R243 | **Subtitle files next to the video are listed but never played (#35, from #33 J). Not built.** The Media Info page lists them (`MediaInfo.subtitlesLine`, Packages/VideoPlayerCore/Sources/AppLogic/MediaInfo.swift: "files: ..."), but `ExternalSubtitleMatcher` (Sources/Subtitles/ExternalSubtitleMatcher.swift: .srt, .ass, .ssa, .vtt, .sup, .sub/.idx; tiers same name, same episode, only video in the folder) has no caller outside its tests, so no sidecar file reaches the player's Subtitles menu. What the player does today: embedded text tracks (SRT, ASS/SSA, WebVTT) are served as WebVTT renditions in the HLS stream and appear in the system subtitle menu; embedded PGS is drawn by the app's own overlay and menu (RemuxEngine/SubtitleRendition.swift, decisions D1 and D5). **Apple:** AVFoundation "Selecting subtitles and alternative audio tracks" (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/avfoundation/selecting-subtitles-and-alternative-audio-tracks): subtitles and closed captions are the `legible` media-selection group of the asset, chosen with `AVPlayerItem.select(_:in:)`, and AVPlayer selects by the person's system preferences by default; the article only covers options the asset itself contains, so a sidecar file has to become part of the stream (as the embedded text tracks do) to appear in that menu. HIG Playing video (https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/playing-video): on tvOS the system player's transport controls are where people turn on subtitles and change the audio language, and it advises using the system player; it says nothing about subtitle file formats. The parsers for these formats exist (SRTParser, ASSParser, WebVTTParser, PGS, VobSubIndex); whether reading a sidecar from every source kind works is unverified. | PROVISIONAL, open: no decision from the owner, nothing built. | (a) wire the matcher into the player's subtitle selection, text formats first (SRT, VTT, ASS/SSA as WebVTT renditions, the same path as embedded text, so they appear in the system menu Apple describes), then image formats (PGS/SUP through the existing picture overlay, VobSub only if it can be drawn): the only option that makes the listed files usable; costs reading one more file per playback from the source and a device check per source kind; (b) text formats only, and the Media Info page says image files (.sup, .sub/.idx) are not used: most of the benefit for less work, honest about the rest, and the picture path can follow later; (c) leave the files listed on Media Info and say on that page that they are not used: no player work and no risk to playback, but the files stay useless and the listing invites a question the app cannot answer |

| R261 | **Export compliance for TestFlight: `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption` (the owner's declaration to Apple; follows R86 / D7, still NOT decided).** project.yml sets it to `false` (unchanged). The app carries its own SSH (Citadel, swift-nio-ssh, swift-crypto) for SFTP, plus HTTPS through the system and SMB encryption in libsmb2 (docs/APPSTORE_READINESS.md section 2). Apple's pages, read 20 Nov 2026: "Complying with Encryption Export Regulations" (developer.apple.com/documentation/security/complying-with-encryption-export-regulations): an app that "uses, accesses, contains, implements, or incorporates encryption" is an export of encryption software; set the key to NO "if your app—including any third-party libraries it links against—doesn't use encryption, or if it only uses forms of encryption that are exempt", "Otherwise, set it to YES"; "Typically, the use of encryption that's built into the operating system ... is exempt", "whereas the use of proprietary encryption is not"; with exempt encryption "you might alternatively be required to submit a year-end self-classification report to the U.S. government". App Store Connect Help "Export compliance documentation for encryption": encryption "limited to that within the Apple operating system": no documentation; "an industry standard algorithm, not provided within the Apple operating system": upload the French encryption declaration, which "is only required if you're distributing your app on the App Store in France"; "proprietary encryption algorithms not accepted by international standard bodies": CCATS plus the French declaration. "Overview of export compliance" lists "Standard encryption algorithms" among the cases that need a determination and names no other exemption list. "ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption" key page: YES means "your app uses non-exempt encryption" and you "typically also provide" `ITSEncryptionExportComplianceCode` (a code Apple gives after reviewing the documents); with no key App Store Connect asks the questions at every upload. "Provide export compliance information for beta builds": "Specify encryption use for your build to avoid the beta being marked as Missing Compliance"; it does not say whether testers can install a Missing Compliance build. The SSH code uses standard algorithms (SSH is an IETF protocol) implemented in the app, not by the operating system, so on Apple's table it sits in the "industry standard algorithm, not provided within the Apple operating system" row (my reading). Mass-market and other U.S. exemption categories are NOT on the Apple pages read (unverified). Not legal advice; this is the owner's declaration to Apple. | PROVISIONAL: not decided, value unchanged (`false`). | (a) keep `false`: no questions, the build is not marked Missing Compliance; it declares no non-exempt encryption, which the owner must be able to stand behind for the bundled SSH code (the year-end self-classification report may apply); (b) `true` and answer App Store Connect's questions: the questions decide whether documents are needed; until they are answered the build is "Missing Compliance"; for standard algorithms Apple's table asks only the French declaration, and only for the App Store in France; after any documents are approved, Apple gives a code for `ITSEncryptionExportComplianceCode` (Apple: about two business days); (c) remove the key: App Store Connect asks the questions at every upload (Manage next to the build); (d) ship without SFTP's own SSH (no Citadel stack) so only the system's encryption remains: removes a feature |

Logs: result and error lines only, redacted twice. `.github/workflows/testflight.yml` is unchanged and is the documented fallback (docs/TESTFLIGHT.md). The pre-push check gained a codemagic.yaml section that holds the file to this one purpose (no exception: the file passed the existing scans). Update (owner, 20 Nov 2026), certificate pile-up RESOLVED: the first version made a private key at run time, so every build would have made a NEW Apple Distribution certificate. The owner has ONE Apple Distribution certificate, stored in Codemagic under the reference name "Balance Ahead distribution" (type production, expires 12 November 2027), which already signs the owner's other two apps; the workflow now reuses exactly that one and NEVER creates, generates, revokes or deletes a certificate or a key. First form: `codemagic.yaml` `ios_signing: certificates: - "Balance Ahead distribution"` (by reference name). Update (20 Nov 2026), Codemagic's validator rejected that form when the owner opened "Specify build configuration" (no build started, no minutes used): "1 validation error in codemagic.yaml: tvos-testflight -> environment -> ios_signing -> Either distribution profile and bundle identifier or provisioning profiles (and optionally certificates) must be provided." Now: `ios_signing: distribution_type: app_store` + `bundle_identifier: com.liamp.videoplayer`, nothing else. Codemagic's signing page (read again 20 Nov 2026) confirms the form: it fetches "all uploaded signing files matching a specific distribution type and bundle identifier", "any uploaded certificates and profiles matching the extensions as well", and with these two fields "it is not allowed to configure `provisioning_profiles` and `certificates` fields". So Codemagic installs the owner's stored distribution certificate by type and bundle identifier; the signing step then checks that exactly one App Store Connect certificate matches the keychain's identity, otherwise it stops. The signing step (`scripts/cm-testflight.sh signing`, helpers in `scripts/cm-signing.py`) finds that certificate in App Store Connect by comparing the SHA-1 of each listed certificate with the keychain's signing identity (`app-store-connect certificates list --json`, `security find-identity`; no key needed, nothing printed but result lines), finds each Bundle ID (`bundle-ids list --strict-match-identifier`), downloads the active TVOS_APP_STORE profiles (`bundle-ids profiles --type TVOS_APP_STORE --state ACTIVE`, `profiles get --save`) and REUSES one that carries the certificate, is App Store, tvOS, and valid for more than a day; only when there is none it makes a PROFILE (`profiles create BUNDLE_ID_RESOURCE_ID --certificate-ids ... --type TVOS_APP_STORE`; the CLI page lists no key option for it). Existing profiles and certificates are left alone: nothing is revoked or deleted anywhere. The pre-push check now allows `ios_signing` with ONLY `distribution_type: app_store` and `bundle_identifier: com.liamp.videoplayer` (it refuses `certificates`, `provisioning_profiles`, another type or bundle identifier, both forms mixed, a missing section) and refuses, in `codemagic.yaml` and `scripts/cm-*`, every command that could make, revoke or delete a key or a certificate (`genrsa`, `openssl req`, `certificates create/revoke/delete`, `fetch-signing-files`, `--create`, `--certificate-key`, `CERTIFICATE_PRIVATE_KEY`, `profiles delete`, and more); `scripts/test-ci-codemagic.sh` checks this. Step order (20 Nov 2026): signing, profile check, prepare, archive, export. Signing and the profile check read only App Store Connect and the keychain (no generated project, no `build/`, no vendored packages), so a signing failure stops the build in about 2 minutes (an estimate, not measured) instead of after the package setup. Why not `fetch-signing-files` without a key: Codemagic's alternative-methods page says that with the integration "the certificate private key has to be exported too" (`CERTIFICATE_PRIVATE_KEY`). UNVERIFIED (never run): NEW RISK, whether Codemagic fails or only warns when no tvOS profile is stored for the bundle identifier (no tvOS profiles are stored in Codemagic; the signing step after it makes them), and whether it then still installs the certificate (the page does not say; if not, the signing step stops with "no code signing identity"), with alternative (e) as the fallback; that the stored certificate lands in the keychain that `security find-identity` searches; the CLI's JSON layout (the readers accept several shapes); that `profiles get --save` and `profiles create --save` honour `--profiles-dir`; that the CLI makes tvOS App Store profiles this way, what `use-profiles` sets, the Codemagic button names marked so in docs/TESTFLIGHT.md, whether the free minutes have reset (R105). Your app is missing the 'aps-environment' entitlement, which is required by tvOS apps that contain the 'com.apple.developer.icloud-services' entitlement." Fix: the owner enabled Push Notifications on the App ID com.liamp.videoplayer and made the app's profile again (Codemagic reference `video_player_tvos_3`, now with aps-environment); `project.yml` adds `aps-environment: production` to the APP's entitlements only (entitlement only, no push code or registration; the Top Shelf extension has no iCloud and gets nothing). | R271 | **Plex playback routes (docs/PLEX_SOURCE.md section 5).** Chosen, in order: (1) direct play of the Part for `mp4`, `m4v`, `mov` with codecs Apple lists (AVPlayer: "local and remote file-based media … as well as … HTTP Live Streaming", read; AVFileType lists mov, mp4, m4v, read; HLS spec: H.264, HEVC, Dolby Vision profile 5, AV1; AC-3, E-AC-3, E-AC-3 JOC, ALAC, FLAC; WebVTT or IMSC1, read), token as a header first, query parameter as fallback (Plex: X-Plex headers may be sent as query arguments, read); (2) MKV and WebM through the app's existing RemuxEngine over ranged reads of the Part (same quality as WebDAV; DTS and TrueHD decoded to FLAC as today); (3) Plex's HLS transcode only as a stated fallback for codecs the app cannot play (MPEG-2, VC-1) or servers without ranges, shown as "Converted by the server". UNVERIFIED: the transcoder endpoint and parameters (not in the text read), Range support on Part URLs, which token form AVPlayer needs, external subtitle streams. The Plex Support direct-play page returned HTTP 403 (not read). | PROVISIONAL: plan only. | (chosen) direct play, then own remux, then server transcode as fallback; (a) direct play and own remux only, no server transcode (the existing media-server rule "direct play only"); (b) server transcode (or Direct Stream) for everything not MP4/MOV, no own remux (less code, lower quality for DTS/TrueHD and Dolby Vision) |
| R272 | **Plex watched state and resume (docs/PLEX_SOURCE.md section 6).** Chosen: read once, then local only. When an item is first indexed, the server's resume position and watched flag seed the app's WatchState only if the app has no record for it; afterwards the app's own records (synced through iCloud) are the truth and nothing is written to the server. Reason: the timeline and scrobble operations were read as names only ("Report media timeline", "Mark an item as played", "Mark an item as unplayed"), and a wrong write would change the owner's Plex history on other devices. A test fails on any write request to the fake server. Stages (section 8): 0 owner decisions R267 to R272; 1 SourcePlex skeleton and fake server; 2 PIN sign-in; 3 servers; 4 browse and index (Plex unblocked then; the tests that assert Plex is blocked change in that stage); 5 direct play and remux; 6 watch seed; 7 later items each after its own decision. WebDAV is untouched in every stage. | PROVISIONAL: plan only. | (chosen) seed once, then local only; (a) fully read-only, never seed; (b) two-way: report progress with the timeline call and mark watched at 95 % with scrobble (after recorded fixtures prove the parameters) |
| R273 | **Add-source forms show no text entry fields (owner's TestFlight report, 20 Nov 2026 (the owner's local date), build of main at 8e347e0, real Apple TV): the WebDAV form showed no fields at all.** Read-only investigation, nothing in the app changed. The screens: Add Source (10) lists the types; each type opens `SourceFormView` (App/Features/Sources/SourceFormView.swift): `TaskPage` (a system `Form`, App/Shared/SettingsPane.swift) with a header, one Section of `SourceFieldRow`s (a `Picker` when the spec has choices, otherwise `FieldRow`), an optional Server Details section, then Connect (prominent) and Cancel and Learn More. WebDAV's specs (SourceCatalog, Packages/VideoPlayerCore/Sources/AppLogic/SourceSetup.swift) are Name, Address (hint "https://nas.local"), User Name, Password, none with choices, so all four are `FieldRow`s. `FieldRow` (App/Shared/LayoutParts.swift, R265, commit 14aa0cf; R256 before it): the label (`FittingText`) above the field; the field is `TextField("", text:, prompt: Text(hint))` or `SecureField` for Password, `.autocorrectionDisabled()`, `.accessibilityLabel(title)`, `.textFieldStyle(.bordered)`, `.textInputBorderShape(.roundedRectangle)`, full width, at least the button height; the row has `.listRowBackground(Color.clear)`. VERIFIED from the code: each field is in the view hierarchy, has no `.disabled`, no `.focusable(false)` and no `.allowsHitTesting(false)`, so it is a focusable text input that opens the system keyboard on select; the row draws no background of its own (cleared); the only shape at rest is whatever the bordered style draws; nothing sets the first focus, and the last hierarchy we have (fixture 11-form-webdav, before R265) shows the focus starting on Connect, below the fields. At rest the user should see: the label, then the field with its grey hint (the prompt) inside a bordered rounded rectangle; focused: the system's focused field look. UNVERIFIED (never seen on a real Apple TV, and no simulator screenshot run since 14aa0cf): what `.bordered` with `.roundedRectangle` draws at rest on tvOS 27 (R265 already marked its fill unverified); whether the hint is drawn at rest; whether the fields focus and open the keyboard on the device (the owner did not say whether pressing up from Connect reaches them; earlier runs R256 pictures 94 and 66 did show focused fields). **Cause, from the code:** (d) excluded (the four fields are in the tree; the pre-R265 hierarchy lists them at 595x73); (b) most likely: the R265 bordered style draws no visible shape at rest on tvOS, so with the row background cleared there is nothing but the labels, which is exactly symptom (a) again (R265 was the attempt to fix (a), and it was never checked in a screenshot); (c) possible as a contributor, not proven: focus starts on Connect, so the user may never land on a field and see it light up. **Proposed smallest fix (not built):** in `FieldRow`, (1) remove `.listRowBackground(Color.clear)` so each field row gets the system Form row platter, a visible shape at rest with the system focus look; (2) keep the label above and give the field the automatic style (drop `.textFieldStyle(.bordered)` and `.textInputBorderShape`); (3) in `SourceFormView`, put the first focus on the first field (`@FocusState` + `.defaultFocus`) so the form opens on Name; (4) before any TestFlight build, run the screenshot job and read 11-form-webdav-FIELDS-AT-REST. Native components only, no custom fill (R165). Risk: whether the row platter draws at rest inside `TaskPage`'s Form on tvOS 27 is unverified; the new check below is what proves it. **Test added (would have caught it):** `ScreenRules.fieldShapeAtRest` (Packages/VideoPlayerCore/Sources/ScreenChecks/FieldShape.swift): for each field without the focus, the dominant colour of strips inside the field near its top and bottom edges against strips just outside it (contrast ratio at least `fieldShapeMinimumRatio`, 1.1), or at least half of a 3 pt edge ring different from the page (an outline); the prompt in the middle never counts; no frame fails; nothing to check fails. 9 Linux tests (FieldShapeTests: no shape fails and names all four fields, prompt alone fails, fill passes, outline passes, near-invisible fill fails, focused field skipped, missing field fails, scale 2, WebDAV frames inside the safe area). UITests/ScreenshotTests.swift now runs it on every source form 11 to 19 at the default size (`fieldsAtRest`, note "<name>-FIELDS-AT-REST", fails the test; WebDAV must have at least one field) and runs `formSafeArea` on 11 at the default size too (before: only under the larger text variant). UNVERIFIED: the UI test has not run (no build here); the 1.1 threshold against real tvOS pixels. **Update (owner, 20 Nov 2026): the chosen fix is BUILT** (provisional, one stage). What changed: `FieldRow` (App/Shared/LayoutParts.swift) no longer clears the row background (the system Form row background is back) and no longer sets `.textFieldStyle(.bordered)` or `.textInputBorderShape`, so the field has the automatic style; the label stays above the field at every text size, the hint stays the prompt, the field stays at least 66 pt high, no custom fill. `FieldRow` and `SourceFieldRow` take an optional focus binding (`OptionalFocus`, a `focused(_:)` only when one is given); `SourceFormView` binds the FIRST field of the form (`@FocusState firstFieldFocused`) and sets `.defaultFocus($firstFieldFocused, true)` on the page (Apple, defaultFocus(_:_:priority:), tvOS 16.0+: focus goes to the given value "when the view is first presented"; priority `automatic`, so the user's own moves are not redirected). The other `FieldRow` users (Enter Key / Replace Key 36, the Move cover 66) get the same row background and field style and no default focus. Safe area and Larger Text, from the code: the row background changes no size and default focus moves no row, so Connect and Cancel stay where R265 put them, and the Larger Text layout (label above the field) is unchanged; both are checked by `formSafeArea` (WebDAV at the default size, test02; Larger Text, accessibility job). The pixel check is fixed for the platter: `ScreenRules.FieldAtRest` has an optional `row` (the Form cell around label and field; the UI test takes the smallest cell that contains the field) and the page colour is sampled just left and right of the row (above and below it when the sides are off the picture), so a field on the platter, or the platter as the only shape, passes; without a row frame it compares just outside the field as before. 5 new Linux tests (field on a platter passes; platter as the only shape passes with the row and fails without it, the false NO SHAPE this removes; a row with no platter and no field shape fails and names all four fields; the focused first field is skipped; a row at the picture's edge uses above and below), 14 in FieldShapeTests. Apple pages read 20 Nov 2026: TextFieldStyle (the `automatic` style is "based on the text field's context"; the page does not say what it draws on tvOS or inside a Form row), defaultFocus(_:_:priority:) (as quoted), HIG Text fields (hint and separate label; "No additional considerations for tvOS"; nothing on a field's shape at rest). STILL UNVERIFIED (nothing compiled for tvOS here, no UI test run, never seen on a device): that the row background draws a visible shape at rest inside `TaskPage`'s Form on tvOS 27; what the automatic field draws inside it, and whether focused it gives two shapes (field and row) or one; that the first focus lands on Name (the 11-form-webdav-FIELDS-AT-REST note prints where the focus is); that Select on a field opens the system keyboard; that Connect and Cancel stay inside the 60 pt safe area; that the cell the UI test picks is the row; the 1.1 threshold on real pixels. Read 20 Nov 2026: XcodeGen ProjectSpec (https://github.com/yonaskolb/XcodeGen/blob/master/Docs/ProjectSpec.md) lists `CFBundleShortVersionString` and `CFBundleVersion` among the properties "generated automatically if appropriate" but does NOT state their default values, nor whether `properties` override them, nor whether `$(...)` references are kept: what the generated plist contained ("1" was seen in TestFlight) is UNVERIFIED from documentation. Apple CFBundleVersion (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/information-property-list/cfbundleversion): "one to three period-separated integers", "only numeric characters (0-9) and periods", required by the App Store; it states no length or digit limit, so a 12-digit value such as 202611210145 is UNVERIFIED until an upload is accepted. Apple "Preparing your app for distribution": with "Manage version and build number" off (our export, `manageAppVersionAndBuildNumber: false`) "you need to manage the build string yourself". That an extension's version must match its containing app: not on the pages read (UNVERIFIED); both targets get the same values anyway. Fix (built): project.yml `info.properties` of the app AND the Top Shelf extension: `CFBundleVersion: "$(CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION)"`, `CFBundleShortVersionString: "$(MARKETING_VERSION)"`; `scripts/cm-testflight.sh archive` now runs `check-build` after `xcodebuild archive` and before export: it reads CFBundleVersion from the archived app's and the Top Shelf extension's Info.plist (PlistBuddy) and stops with one error line per bundle that differs from the build number; tests in scripts/test-ci-codemagic.sh (fixture archives: matching, "1" in one target, missing key, missing extension; project.yml keys parsed). The audit's earlier citation of the 35 % dimming layer is withdrawn: on the Materials page that advice is for components with CLEAR Liquid Glass over bright content, not for a page background. The pages' text starts at the top, so the material covers the whole picture (the sample's below-the-fold state); the sample's above-the-fold look (the top of the picture clear, the content in the lower part) needs the content moved: alternative (b), for the owner. T8: a cast member with no photo (today everyone from the online lookup, which requests no photos) showed the system person symbol; now their initials (`Monogram.initials`, AppLogic, Linux-tested) in the same circle on the system quaternary fill. Not changed, with reasons (docs/HIG_AUDIT.md status): T7, the second focus request on Play, fixes a measured run (#25 B10: a page pushed at launch kept the tab bar's focus); T9, Media Info is already text, not a collection; T10, a season picker changes the page's structure and the TV app's pattern is not on any page read (R338). Selecting a cast member does nothing (no person page; R182, R338). UI notes `57-movie-detail-CONTRAST` and `55-show-page-CONTRAST` (every text against the new background; notes, not failures, as R240); pictures 55, 57, 58, 60, 61, 77 show both changes (names unchanged). | R340 | **Player: Episodes and Playback Stats tabs in the info panel, the system Up Next, and the next episode really starting (docs/HIG_AUDIT.md T15 to T19; owner decision of 21 Nov 2026 in R338).** (1) Episodes: a content tab of the system player's info panel listing the season that is playing as 16:9 lockups (`PlayerEpisodesPanel`, AppLogic `PlayerEpisodes`); choosing another episode plays it, the one playing says "Now Playing". (2) Playback Stats: the overlay over the picture (`contentOverlayView`) and its transport-bar item are gone; the stats are a content tab (`StatsPanelView`), every stat shown in three columns, most important first (`StatsOverlay.panelRows`: picture, then sound, then whether playback keeps up, then technical detail; tested to keep every row), the honest status lines above; updated once a second while the tab is on screen. Settings > Playback > Show Playback Stats in the Player now adds or removes the tab (footer reworded). (3) Up Next: the system's own (`AVContentProposal` on the item, the card an `AVContentProposalViewController` subclass `UpNextProposalController` with Play Now and Cancel as system buttons and the video at 960 x 540 as Apple's article does). The playback logic runs in Ask mode and never counts down itself; with Auto-Play Next Episode on, `automaticAcceptanceInterval` makes the system accept 10 s after the card appears (`UpNextCard.acceptanceInterval`), off it is NaN. (4) The bug: `PlayerCoordinator.handle` let `.advance` fall into `default: break`, so the next episode never started; now it plays the next item through `PlayerScreen` (the old playback stops first: position saved, the Plex watch sync gets its stop, the next one its start), resuming at its saved position. The overlay's effects reached `handle` twice (returned and through the sink); now once. Picture subtitles (T18) unchanged. Sources, read 21 Nov 2026: `customInfoViewControllers` ("The system uses a view controller's title property value as the content tab title"; "The view with the greatest height determines the height of all of the content views", so both tabs ask for one height); HIG Playing video ("content tabs, like Info, Episodes, or Chapters"; "Defer to content when displaying logos or noninteractive overlays above video"); `AVContentProposal`, `automaticAcceptanceInterval` ("The interval between the time playback ends and automatic acceptance"; "Set this value to nan to disable"), the article "Presenting content proposals in tvOS" (the card subclass, `preferredPlayerViewFrame`, -1.0 used as an interval), and `playerViewController(_:didAccept:)` ("Implement this method to replace the player's current player item"). Tests (Linux): `PlayerPanelsTests` (the interval, the countdown from the playhead, Ask mode never advances by itself and advances once on accept), `StatsPanelOrderTests`. UI (test12): note 24-player-info-panel-TABS, new pictures 24-player-episodes-tab, 24-player-stats-tab and 27-player-after-play-now with the note -ADVANCE (plan 177). UNVERIFIED until the next compile run: everything compiles for tvOS (parse-app is a syntax check); the tabs' look and height on tvOS 27; whether the system's automatic acceptance calls `didAccept`; whether the system draws its own countdown on a custom card; the jump between two players on advance (the screen shows the loading page between them). | PROVISIONAL: built, not yet compiled for tvOS or run (the owner decided to build it). | (chosen) as described; (a) the app's own countdown with the system card's countdown off (before R340); (b) replace the player item inside one player (`replaceCurrentItem`, Apple's article) in place of a new player per item, which keeps one controller but needs the coordinator rebuilt per item; (c) stats as a list of rows in one column (the panel would need scrolling); (d) the stats tab only when the setting is on, the overlay kept as an option |
| R341 | **Cast member page: the library's titles with one person (docs/HIG_AUDIT.md T8; owner decision of 21 Nov 2026 in R338).** Each entry of the cast shelf on the movie and show pages is now a borderless lockup (`NavigationLink` to `Route.person`, `.buttonStyle(.borderless)`, the system highlight on the circle) in place of a focusable entry with no action. It opens `PersonView`: the person's name, the count ("6 titles") and the same poster grid and cards as Movies and Shows (`PosterGridLayout`, `PosterCard`, `SeriesCard`), movies first, then shows, each in title order; "No Titles" when none. The titles come from AppLogic `PersonTitles`: every movie and show in the library whose stored cast lists the name (compared without case, accents or extra spaces). Nothing is asked online: `OnlineServices.storedDetails()` builds the same Wikidata and TMDB layers as the pages, but on `NoNetworkTransport` (every request fails before any network) with no retries, so the clients answer only from the stored copies already in the metadata cache (`TMDBClient.fetch` and `WikidataClient.lookup` return the stored copy, fresh or old, when the network fails). The media server's own layer is left out, since asking it would be a request. Sources: HIG Lockups ("Monograms identify people, usually the cast and crew"), HIG Layout (the grid spacing of docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md section 4); the TV app opening a person's page from its cast row is from the owner's decision, not read on an Apple page. Tests (Linux): `PersonTitlesTests` (name comparison, the stored client answers from an old cached copy and fails without sending for a title not stored, one attempt only, the sample library's Sample Actor 1 in five movies and one show). UI (test13): picture 79-person-page and its note -TITLES (plan 178). UNVERIFIED until the next compile run: everything compiles for tvOS (parse-app is a syntax check); the page's look on tvOS 27; how long the page takes on a large library (one stored read per title). | PROVISIONAL: built, not yet compiled for tvOS or run (the owner decided to build it). | (chosen) as described; (a) match by the TMDB person id where both sides have one, names only for Wikidata (more exact, but the Wikidata layer gives no id); (b) keep each title's cast in the library index when the page is shown, so the person page is one query (needs a schema change); (c) the movie and show grids as two shelves under headings in place of one grid; (d) leave the media server's layer in (Plex titles then show their server cast, at the price of a request to the server) |

Now the poster is a `NavigationLink` (still the system `.card` button style) to `DiscoverTitlePage`: the poster, the title, year and why it is suggested, "Movie" or "Show", where to watch it, and an Add to Watchlist / Remove from Watchlist button. | R345 | **Room for the focus effect: no focused row is clipped (owner's top priority after the compile run of 21 Nov 2026 on a0ae4a6).** The run's pictures showed every focused row of the two-column Settings and Files pages cut flat on its left side at x 990, the left edge of the right-hand column (31 "Play Next Episode Automatically" x 990 to 1843 while the unfocused rows are 990 to 1839; 32, 33 "Add a Source", 35, 36, 37, 38, 41 scrolled, 64 Needs Review and its Larger Text twins, 69, 71 to 73, 105, 109 and the Larger Text and Bold Text twins 83, 88, 92, 98): the system focus effect grows a focused row a few points on each side, and the `Form` / `List` in `PaneColumns` clipped the growth to its own bounds. The task pages (10 to 19, 21, 59, 66) were whole (their Form spans two thirds of the screen with its own inner margins). Cause found with a pixel scan of all 170 pictures of the run: in a cut row the left edge reaches its extreme column in 38 pixel rows and the right edge in 20 (a rounded corner reaches it only in the middle rows, the same number on both sides). Fix, the system way: `scrollClipDisabled()` (tvOS 17.0+, "Sets whether a scroll view clips its content to its bounds") on every container that holds focusable rows: `PaneColumns`' right column (every Settings page, Files, Needs Review, Move, How This Was Sorted), `SettingsPane`'s Form, `TaskPage`'s Form (all task covers: Add a Source, the forms, Your Network, File Details, Save View, Fix Match), the Files lists, the Full Licence Texts list, Home's shelves, the movie and show pages' scroll views (the horizontal shelves already had it), Discover and the player's Episodes tab (already had it). No `clipped()` / `clipShape` / `mask` on any container of rows; the system focus effect is unchanged. Guards: design-lint rule `focus-room` (a container without `scrollClipDisabled()`, or a clipping modifier in such a file; two allowed exceptions, long text pages and the cast photo circle); the UI tests run `FocusRoom` on EVERY picture (`shot`): the white platter of a focused row must have both rounded sides intact (flat-row counts within 8) and lie inside the picture, else a -FOCUS-CLIPPED note and a test failure; proven in `FocusRoomTests` (synthetic whole and cut rows, and on the run's pictures: 31 fails, 10-add-source-types passes). Screens changed: every Settings page (30 to 38, 40 to 43), Files (70 to 73), Needs Review (64, 65), Move (66), How This Was Sorted (67), sorting screens (69), the task covers (10 to 21, 59, 74), Home, the movie and show pages. UNVERIFIED until the next compile run: that a `Form` / `List` (a collection view underneath) honours `scrollClipDisabled()` on tvOS 27 (Apple's page shows a `ScrollView`); that the rows of a scrolled list do not then draw over the page title when they scroll up (the column starts below the tab bar; the next 41/43 scrolled pictures show it). | PROVISIONAL: built, not yet compiled for tvOS or run. | (chosen) `scrollClipDisabled()` on the containers; (a) `contentMargins(.horizontal, ..., for: .scrollContent)` so the rows sit inside the list with room for the growth (a number of ours, the lint's hard-padding rule needs a HIG source); (b) a wider right column so the list starts left of the row (the same clipping at the new edge); (c) `.listStyle(.grouped)` defaults with no column (rows edge to edge, against L1). |
| R346 | **Up on a long page scrolls back to the first row before the tab bar (owner's device test of build 5, 21 Nov 2026, item A).** On a long Settings page, Up from a lower row jumped to the tab bar as soon as the focus reached the top of the visible area; tvOS Settings and the TV app scroll the page back row by row and reach the tab bar only from the first row. Fix: every scrolling page is one focus region, `focusSection()` (tvOS 15.0+): the right column of `PaneColumns` (every Settings pane, About, Needs Review, Files), `TaskPage`'s Form, the licence list, the movie page and the show page. | R348 | **Pressing the selected tab again goes back to its first page (owner's device test of build 5, 21 Nov 2026, item B).** On build 5, pressing Settings again left the pushed Settings page in place. | R349 | **Home rebuilt as the TV app's Home: a featured title, then shelves (owner's device test of build 5, 21 Nov 2026, item C).** Reference: the Home screen of Apple's TV app on tvOS 27, from the owner's written description and apple.com/apple-tv-app (the owner's photos replace it when they come; docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md "Reference screens"). Top: a full-width featured area, two thirds of the screen high (`HomeFeaturedLayout.heightFraction`), the title's large artwork (the show's for an episode) full strength at the top with the system material rising over its lower part (`BackdropStyle.featuredClearTop` 0.35), the title logo or the name (`TitleHeader`, the Title Logos setting applies), ONE metadata line (the card's second line, then its format badges, dropped first; rule S1), Play (Resume for a started title) and More (the title's page; the show's page for an episode). | R351 | **Readable text on focused rows and pages (compile run of 21 Nov 2026 on a0ae4a6, item 2).** What the 1.2 to 1.3:1 "text" was: not a text colour. The -CONTRAST notes measured the whole ROW's frame (1133 pt wide) for a one-word label ("Name", "Server", "Port", "Link", "Year"); the glyphs covered less than the 3 % of the frame the measure took as ink, so its median fell on the row platter's own shading (12-form-smb "Port" 1.22:1, #292929 on #181818). The measure now takes the glyph cores among the pixels that differ from the background (`Contrast.backgroundTolerance` 24, `coreFraction` 0.25; `ContrastRowTests`, synthetic and on the run's pictures): the same labels measure 17.46 to 20.82:1 (12 Name, Server, Port; 19 Link; 66 Year), and the 36 status lines still measure 4.29 and 4.30:1, so they were real. Fixes, system styles only: a text entry row's value is no longer forced to `.secondary` (it stayed light grey on the white focused row); the system's value style in a Form applies, and the system changes it with the focus (`TextEntryPage`). The movie page's year and runtime line and quality line (57: 4.38 and 4.44:1) take `ReadableSecondary` in R350's one metadata line. The initials of a cast member without a photo (S2, S4, S6: 3.53 to 3.56:1) take the primary label colour. UNVERIFIED until the next run's -CONTRAST notes (11 to 19, 34, 36, 37, 57, 66). | PROVISIONAL (targets of R240: 4.5:1 for text, 7:1 for titles on covers). | (chosen) the system hierarchical levels: primary, and `ReadableSecondary` (R240) for secondary text; (a) primary for every status line (no level below the row titles); (b) keep `.secondary` and accept below 4.5:1 for secondary text (rejected: the HIG's contrast table asks 4.5:1 for text of this size). Measure: (chosen) glyph cores among the non-background pixels; (a) measure the text's own frame from the tree (a label's frame in a row is often the row's). |

| R354 | **A long lockup caption no longer runs over the line under it (compile run of 21 Nov 2026 on a0ae4a6, item 5; follows R264).** Pictures 55, 56, 77 and 108: S2 E3's long title was drawn on three lines over its "43 min" line. The run's hierarchy (55-show-page-HIERARCHY) gives the cause as far as the run shows it: the caption's text was laid out 30 pt high (one line) but drawn 88 pt high (three lines), and "43 min" sat 42 pt under the caption's top as on the one-line cards. `CardCaption` chose between the one-line and the wrapping text with a `@State` flag set by a hidden measure AFTER the first layout; the text switched, but the card did not take the new height. Why the later pass did not move the lines under it (the tvOS borderless lockup lays out its own label) is not on Apple's pages: UNVERIFIED. Now: `CardCaption` is a system `ViewThatFits` (tvOS 16.0+) of three children, one Caption line, one Caption 2 line (the 23 pt floor), then Caption 2 wrapping onto as many lines as needed; the choice is made in the same layout pass, with no measured state. The shrink is in two steps instead of continuous. UI check: `captionOverlapCheck` (note "-OVERLAP" on 50 and 56) fails when another text of the card intersects the caption. UNVERIFIED until a run: that the runtime now sits under the three-line caption. Shared with the player's episode panel (App/Features/Player is not changed; it uses the same `CardCaption`). | PROVISIONAL | (a) `ViewThatFits`, stepped shrink (chosen). (b) Keep the continuous shrink and measured switch, and give the caption a fixed reserve of two floor lines (R264 (c); titles over two lines would still overflow). (c) The title and the runtime as ONE `Text` with a line break, so they can never overlap (the runtime would lose its own slot beside the watched mark). (d) Never wrap: one line at the floor, then drop the episode code, then the item page only shows the rest (R264 (f), changes what the card says). |
