| R361 | **Down from the controls above a poster grid reaches a card in any column (compile run of 21 Nov 2026 on a0ae4a6, item 8).** Picture 54-shows-focused-NOT-REACHED: the focus stopped on "Jump to Letter" (x 785 to 1108), with the only two show cards at x 80 to 760 below it; the note "No sample card took the focus after Down". The cause is the focus engine's search, not the Shows layout: Down looks for a focusable view below the focused one, and none was under the button. Movies fills more columns, so the same move found a card there. Fix: `.focusSection()` on `PosterGridLayout` (every poster grid: Movies, Shows, the person page). Apple, `focusSection()`: "Indicates that the view's frame and cohort of focusable descendants should be used to guide focus movement"; with it "the view's frame becomes capable of accepting focus in order to direct it at its nearest focusable descendant in the direction of travel". Also `SeriesCard` now has the artwork and text as siblings in its label, as R360 did for `PosterCard` (missed in that commit). The 54 note names this fix if the card is still not reached. (b) Move "Jump to Letter" to the left of the row (a layout change to work around the search; other controls could end up in the same place). (c) `defaultFocus` on the first card (sets where focus starts when the screen appears, not where Down goes from a control). (d) A `focusSection()` on the controls row too (needed only if Up from a card misses the row; not seen). |
| R362 | **The page behind a task cover is still in the accessibility tree (compile run of 21 Nov 2026 on a0ae4a6, item 10; follows R292).** Run 8: every -COVER note says "PAGE BEHIND THE COVER IS EXPOSED" (07 Saved View: screen.movies and screen.shows; 10 to 21: screen.home; 59: screen.item; 66, 67, 74). The page's controls are listed with isHittable false, and the tab bar is in the tree too, not hittable (10-add-source-types-HIERARCHY). Four hiding modifiers are in place (the TabView, each tab's NavigationStack, each page screen, the isModal trait on the cover), and all three hiding ones read one value, `CoverPresence.isCovered`. **The cause is not proven without a run.** Two causes are left, and the evidence fits both: (1) the views behind stay in the window and XCTest lists them whatever they are told: Apple's `presentationBackground(_:)` page says a presentation background "Allows views behind the presentation to show through translucent styles", and every cover had one; (2) the counter never rose, so no view was told to hide. What changes: `presentationBackground(.background)` is removed from `TaskCoverChrome` (the opaque full-bleed `background(_:ignoresSafeAreaEdges:)` stays, so the cover still covers the screen; pixel check B 1 is the guard). A UI-test-only probe tells the causes apart on the next run: the cover's value reads "cover probe: open covers N" and every page screen's value reads "cover probe: told hidden true/false" (DEBUG and a UI-test launch only, nothing in Release). The -COVER note writes them under "cover counter (R362, record only)". The system fix that stays: the cover's `isModal` trait (Apple: "When a modal accessibility element is visible, sibling accessibility elements that are not modal are ignored"), which acts on VoiceOver, not on XCTest's tree. UNVERIFIED: which cause it is; whether tvOS 27 drops the page from the tree without `presentationBackground`; whether the cover stays opaque without it (B 1); whether VoiceOver can reach the page (XCTest has no VoiceOver query). | PROVISIONAL | (a) Remove `presentationBackground` and add the probe (chosen). (b) Keep `presentationBackground` and change only the check, so a page listed with every element not hittable passes (it matches the system's isModal behaviour, but the check would no longer prove anything about the tree). (c) Remove our counter and the three `accessibilityHidden` modifiers and rely on `isModal` alone (less of our own code; can be done once the probe shows the counter is not the cause). (d) Present task screens with `NavigationStack` pushes instead of covers (the page is then not in the window at all; it reverses R228). |

| R374 | **The source's own content rating now also comes from Jellyfin, Emby and NFO files, fed into R371's layering, source first (owner's review of R370 to R372, 21 Nov 2026, item 2).** Order of the source layer: the media server's own field, then the title's NFO file; then TMDB and Wikidata as R371 and R372. Server fields, read on the clients' API pages on 21 Nov 2026: Plex `contentRating` (as R371); Jellyfin `OfficialRating` on `BaseItemDto` (https://api.jellyfin.org, the stable OpenAPI file: "The official rating", a string); Emby `OfficialRating` on `BaseItemDto` (https://dev.emby.media, ItemsService getItems). The clients keep the field with each file of an item when the item list carries it (`officialRating(at:)`, no request). **Fields, settled from the documentation (owner's review of R374, 21 Nov 2026):** `OfficialRating` is NOT a value the items endpoints' `Fields` parameter takes, so it cannot be added there. Jellyfin: `ItemFields` (the type of `GET /Items` `fields`; OpenAPI 12.4.0) lists AirTime, CanDelete, CanDownload, ChannelInfo, Chapters, Trickplay, ChildCount, CumulativeRunTimeTicks, CustomRating, DateCreated, DateLastMediaAdded, DisplayPreferencesId, Etag, ExternalUrls, Genres, ItemCounts, MediaSourceCount, MediaSources, OriginalTitle, Overview, ParentId, Path, People, PlayAccess, ProductionLocations, ProviderIds, PrimaryImageAspectRatio, RecursiveItemCount, Settings, SeriesStudio, SortName, SpecialEpisodeNumbers, Studios, Taglines, Tags, RemoteTrailers, MediaStreams, SeasonUserData, DateLastRefreshed, DateLastSaved, RefreshState, ChannelImage, EnableMediaSourceDisplay, Width, Height, ExtraIds, LocalTrailerCount, IsHD, SpecialFeatureCount (`OfficialRating` appears only in `ItemSortBy`). Emby: the `Fields` options of `GET /Items` and `GET /Users/{UserId}/Items` are Budget, Chapters, DateCreated, Genres, HomePageUrl, IndexOptions, MediaStreams, Overview, ParentId, Path, People, ProviderIds, PrimaryImageAspectRatio, Revenue, SortName, Studios, Taglines (`OfficialRating` appears only as a filter: MinOfficialRating, MaxOfficialRating, HasOfficialRating, OfficialRatings). Where the docs say it is returned: Emby's Item-Information page, list queries are "stripped to include only a minimal amount of information" and for a single item "the entire object is returned"; Browsing-the-Library names `/Users/{UserId}/Items/{Id}` for that. Jellyfin's `GET /Items/{itemId}` ("Gets an item from a user's library.", `userId` optional) returns `BaseItemDto`. So when the list lacks it, the app asks the single-item endpoint ONCE when the title's page first opens and keeps the answer in the library like the NFO rating (`requestOfficialRating(at:)`, `SourceRatings.storedRating`; a failed request is not kept and is tried again; "" records that the server has none). Emby's endpoint needs the user's id, taken from the sign-in response (`User.Id`; the recorded Emby 4.10 sign-in has `User` as one object, though the reference page types it `UserDto[]`); with a pasted token there is no documented way to learn the user's id (UserService has no current-user endpoint), so no request is made and the NFO file is the fallback. Only the rating: Jellyfin and Emby keep their synopsis and artwork order (R210, `ServerMetadataKinds.used` stays Plex; `ServerMetadataKinds.rating` is Plex, Jellyfin, Emby). NFO: the `.nfo` next to the video with the same name (the library's sidecar link), its `<movie>` or `<episodedetails>` document, `<mpaa>` else `<certification>` (the existing NFO parser), read once when the page is first opened and stored in the library (schema 7, `items.content_rating`; NULL not read, "" read and none; a missing or unreadable file is tried again later); `ContentRating.clean` then tidies "Rated PG-13" and "US:TV-14". Limits: a folder-wide `movie.nfo` or `tvshow.nfo` is not read; a changed NFO is not read again; a show page ("series-N") gets no source rating. UNVERIFIED (the pages do not say): which properties Jellyfin and Emby put in a list response without `Fields` (neither names the base set), so whether the list ever carries `OfficialRating`; Emby's single-item endpoint with a pasted token (no user id). Confirmed in this session: Linux package tests with hand-written FIXTURE items and NFO files (`SourceRatingsTests`, the client tests of both servers including the single-item request): 2316 passed, 133 skipped, 0 failed; parse-app is a syntax check only. Functionality follows the owner's reference player, which reads these ratings today (the owner's standing rule R312, not named). No compile check, build or push (R318); staged by the owner's staging rule (R370). | PROVISIONAL | (a) Server field first, then the NFO file, stored once (chosen). (b) NFO first, then the server (the user's own file wins over the server's copy). (c) Read the NFO during the library sync for every title (no first-open delay, but every title's file read up front). (d) Also read folder-wide `movie.nfo` / `tvshow.nfo` (more coverage, more guessing which title a folder file belongs to). (e) Ask Jellyfin and Emby for the rating with a separate per-item request (chosen after the owner's review of R374: once per title when the list lacks it, kept in the library). (f) Ask on every page open without keeping it (a server change shows at once; one request per page). (g) Emby with a pasted token: ask the server for its users to find the id (`GET /Users/Public` lists only public users; not done). |

| R381 | **Fix Match choices kept in iCloud, matches.json as the local cache (audit docs/AUDIT_AGAINST_REFERENCE_PACK.md #14, section 5; owner 21 Nov 2026, PROVISIONAL under the rule "Apple's published guidance first").** Apple's archived App Programming Guide for tvOS (as quoted by the audit, not re-opened here): 500 KB of persistent local storage (NSUserDefaults), all other data purgeable by the system, data that must last in iCloud. Built with the mechanism the app already uses for the person's own data: the user-data ledger that `CloudKitUserDataCloud` syncs to the private CloudKit database of iCloud.com.liamp.videoplayer (one encrypted record per ledger record). Its schema has had a "fixmatch" record type since the ledger was written; nothing wrote it before. Now AppLogic `CloudMatchStore` writes every save and removal into the file and the ledger (a removal is a tombstone; Undo writes the old choice again, later, so it comes back), and every read reconciles the two with `FixMatchCopies` at most once per 30 s (PROVISIONAL) and always when the file is missing: (1) no file: the iCloud copy, and the file is rebuilt; (2) a choice in both: the iCloud copy wins; (3) a choice only in the file: kept and sent to iCloud, stamped with the time it was made, unless iCloud holds a later removal of the same file, season or folder. The "fixmatch" atomic group now holds every field of a choice (dates in whole seconds, as the file keeps them). `JSONFileMatchStore.replaceAll` rewrites the cache. OnlineServices passes the ledger in; the iCloud backup still includes matches.json. Tests: FixMatchCopiesTests, FixMatchRecordsTests, CloudMatchStoreTests (local files and an in-memory ledger only). **UNVERIFIED:** that iCloud sync carries the records between devices or restores them after a reinstall (only a device with an iCloud account can show it; `CloudKitUserDataCloud` itself is marked never compiled); the ledger file itself is in Caches (user-data-ledger.json), so with iCloud off a purge of Caches leaves only matches.json, which then seeds the ledger again. Other files are not moved (findings for the owner in the report of 21 Nov 2026). | PROVISIONAL (owner's audit decision, 21 Nov 2026) | (a) iCloud through the user-data ledger, matches.json as the cache (chosen). (alert title "Format Not Supported", no Try Again). An unknown codec word is not refused: the player gets the file and reports its own error. | R306 | **Plex stage 6: watch state copied once, never written back (docs/PLEX_SOURCE.md section 6; R272 recommendation applied).** After the first COMPLETE scan of a Plex source, `AppServices` reads the server's state of every file it listed (`viewOffset` in ms, `viewCount`, `lastViewedAt`, the item's `duration`) and `PlexWatchSeed` (AppLogic) copies it into the app's own watch state, only for items the app has recorded nothing for (not watched, position 0, play count 0, never played): played (`viewCount` above 0, no resume point) is marked watched at the server's date; stopped at 30 seconds or more resumes there (a rewatch in progress counts as stopped); under 30 seconds or never played changes nothing. Two files of one item: the one watched last decides. The source then gets the option `seeded=done` (saved like any source change) and is never seeded again; a failed seed is tried again after the next scan. Nothing is ever sent to the server: no timeline, no scrobble, no write of any kind; the end-to-end test lists every request that is not GET or HEAD and fails on any. Docs re-read for this stage: the Library section read 20 Nov 2026 for stage 4 did not contain `viewOffset`, `viewCount` or `lastViewedAt`, so those names stay UNVERIFIED (they come from the hand-written fixtures); the timeline and scrobble pages were not needed, nothing is written. Tests: 3 seed tests on the real in-memory library (watched and resume, the app's own state never overwritten, once per Plex source and only Plex) and 1 end-to-end test (the real scanner indexes the fake Plex server, the seed runs, a second run keeps all 3, no server write). The paths are read once per server from `GET /media/providers` (the library provider's `timeline` feature: `key`, `scrobbleKey`, `unscrobbleKey`, as in the documented example); a missing path, or one that is not a plain path on the same server, falls back to the documented path; a server that cannot be reached throws (the write can be kept for later) and is asked for its paths again next time. `ServerTimelineRhythm` (VideoPlayerCore): a report is due at every state change, otherwise every 10 seconds ("generally this means every 10 seconds on a LAN/WAN, and every 20 seconds over cellular", read); `stopped` is not sent twice. Nothing calls the client yet (stage 2 wires it). | R309 | **Plex two-way watch state, stage 2: the wiring and the switch (R306 b, R308; docs/PLEX_SOURCE.md section 6).** A per-source switch "Sync Watched Status with Plex" in the Plex source's settings (`SourceDetailView`, a system Toggle row with a one-line footer), stored as the source option `watchSync=1`, **off by default**. `ServerWatchRecorder` (AppLogic) sits in front of the ledger recorder: the app's own watched changes (the 95 % rule, the end of the film, Mark as Watched) are passed on once per change from unwatched to watched, and every Mark as Unwatched; a resume point alone is never passed on. `ServerWatchSync` (AppLogic, one ordered queue) sends them: watched → `PUT /:/scrobble`, unwatched → `PUT /:/unscrobble`, for every Plex file of the item (one item key = one film) whose source has the switch on, each server item once. The player reports `/:/timeline` (playing, paused, buffering from `timeControlStatus`; stopped when the player closes, `continuing=0`; one session id per playback) at once on a state change and otherwise every 10 seconds (R308 rhythm; a one-second clock keeps the paused reports going). Played through a Plex file with the switch on: reports go to that file only; played through another source: to every Plex file of the same item whose switch is on (**owner's decision, 21 Nov 2026**: an item on a Plex source and another source counts as the same film, so marking it or playing it through the other source is sent to Plex too). The switch is read at every send, so turning it off stops the next write. With the switch off nothing is sent and the server is not even asked for the item's id. What the one-time seed (R306) copies from the server is not sent back (a task-local "echo" flag around the seed). Stage 2 sends once: a failed request is counted, not retried (stage 3 adds the offline queue). Tests: 10 AppLogic tests with a fake writer (switch default and kinds, switch off sends nothing and asks nothing, watched and unwatched, the 95 % rule sent once, an item only on another source, the seed not sent back, the same film through another source, the 10-second rhythm and state changes, switch off during playback, failures counted) and 2 end-to-end tests through the real Plex client and the fake server (switch off: no write request and no `/media/providers` request; switch on: three timelines, scrobble and unscrobble with the exact query and headers). | R310 | **Plex two-way watch state, stage 3: newer change wins and writes made while the server is off (R306 b, R309; docs/PLEX_SOURCE.md section 6).** (1) **Queue:** a write whose server cannot be reached (no answer at all; a server that answers with a refusal is counted, not kept) waits in `ServerWatchQueue`: per file the newest resume point and the newest watched flag (a Mark as Unwatched replaces a waiting Mark as Watched), with the time of the change. It is a JSON file next to the library database in Caches, holding the source id, the file's path on the server, the kind, a position and a time; no token, no address, not synced. It is sent, oldest first, after the next write that reaches that server and after each complete scan of the source: the resume point as a stopped `/:/timeline` with `offline=1` and `updated` (the time of the change), the flag as scrobble or unscrobble. A waiting change older than the server's `lastViewedAt` for that file is dropped; a switch turned off drops what waits (nothing is ever sent while it is off). (2) **Merge (replaces R306's "copy once" while the switch is on):** after every complete scan of a source with the switch on, what waits is sent first, then per item the app's state is compared with the server's (`PlexWatchMerge`): the same state (both watched, or resume points less than 30 seconds apart) changes nothing; otherwise the newer change wins: the server's `lastViewedAt` against the later of the app's last play and its last Mark as Unwatched (the ledger's play-count epoch); changes less than 1 second apart count as simultaneous and change nothing. The server's newer state is written into the app without being sent back; the app's newer state is sent (watched: scrobble; a resume point: stopped timeline with `offline=1` and `updated`; unwatched: unscrobble). A side that has never recorded anything loses to one that has (as the R306 seed). A server item played with a resume point counts as a rewatch in progress (its resume point counts, as in R306). With the switch off, R306's one-time copy stays exactly as before. Tests: 8 more AppLogic tests (the decision table; kept writes saved across launches and replayed with offline and updated; the next write that reaches the server sends what waited; a waiting change older than the server's last view dropped; switch off drops what waits; after a scan the newer side wins both ways and nothing comes back; a newer Mark as Unwatched sent; the queue file holds no address or token) and 2 end-to-end tests through the real Plex client and the fake server (server off: nothing arrives, then the replay sends `state=stopped`, `time`, `offline=1`, `updated` and unscrobble; the merge takes the fixture's 600-second resume point and writes nothing). Nothing compiled for tvOS; the app wiring is syntax-checked only. UNVERIFIED until a real server: whether Plex accepts `offline=1` with `updated` for a resume point set this way, and the unit of `updated` (seconds are sent); whether `lastViewedAt` changes on unscrobble and on a timeline report (the merge relies on it as the server's change time); clock difference between the Apple TV and the server (1 second tolerance only). | PROVISIONAL: newest-per-file queue in Caches; replay after a reaching write and after each scan; drop a waiting change older than the server's last view; 1-second tolerance; switch off drops the queue; the unwatched time taken from the ledger's play-count epoch. | (chosen) as described; (a) keep every waiting write in order instead of the newest per file and kind; (b) keep the queue in the synced ledger instead of a local file (other Apple TVs would replay it too); (c) on a tie or unknown times, the app always wins (instead of no change); (d) on a tie, the server always wins; (e) replay on a timer while the server is off, instead of after a reaching write and after scans; (f) a waiting change is sent even when the server saw a newer view (app always wins for its own queued changes); (g) keep what waits when the switch is turned off and send it when turned on again |

The last problem (step, source type, container, codecs, error with every token removed, `PlaybackRedaction`) is kept on this Apple TV only and shown in Settings > Playback. UI test 22 now says how a start ended (the alert's text, the spinner's step, or "SILENT FAILURE"). Tests: PlaybackStartLogTests (Linux). | PROVISIONAL: nothing here has run on tvOS; the cause of the hang is not proven until the next build's run of test 22 and the owner's device (the new row then names the step). The limits are first guesses. | (chosen) alert inside the cover, limits per step, diagnostic row in Settings > Playback; (a) keep the alert on the page underneath and raise it after the cover's `onDismiss`; (b) one overall limit for the whole start instead of one per step; (c) the diagnostic in the Playback Stats tab instead (only reachable when the player opens); (d) longer limits, or none for the resume seek (fall back to playing from the start instead of failing). |

| R316 | **Intro and credits found on the Apple TV by comparing the audio of a season's episodes (replaces the plan of R284 for the intro and credits; docs/PLAYBACK_AUTOSKIP_NOWPLAYING.md section 6).** Method: the audio of the intro window (first 10 min or first quarter) and the credits window (last 10 min or 40 %) is read as mono at 11 kHz, reduced to about 5.5 kHz, and each 0.19 s frame becomes one 32-bit word that records how the energy of 33 bands between 300 and 2000 Hz changed (a classic spectral-band fingerprint, own code, no framework). Two episodes are compared at every offset; the longest stretch where words differ in at most 10 of 32 bits (holes up to 3 s, edges trimmed to solid matches) is the shared part: an intro is 15 to 120 s, credits 15 to 300 s; a longer match means the same recording and is ignored. Each episode is compared with up to four others of the same season; the median of the largest group of votes agreeing within 1 s is its segment, with confidence 0.75 after one vote and 0.9 after two (then it skips by itself), up to 0.98. The fingerprints of the newest 8 episodes are kept, the votes of all. Stored as one JSON file per season in the app's caches folder, named by a hash (no title in a file name); nothing is sent anywhere. Read while an episode plays, at background priority, for that episode and the next one. Limits: needs two episodes of one season with the same theme; no recap detection; a cold open is never part of the intro; files the system player cannot open (Matroska through the app's own remux, byte-reader sources) are not analysed; the system may empty the caches folder (then it is learned again). Linux tests on synthetic audio (`AudioDetectionTests`, `SegmentDetectionTests`). UNVERIFIED until a device run: whether `AVAssetReader` reads an HTTP file here at all, the time and network cost on an Apple TV, the accuracy on real episodes. | PROVISIONAL: tuning values (bit errors, holes, lengths, confidences) are first guesses. | (chosen) audio comparison within a season, stored in caches; (a) also credits by silence, black frames and rolling text (R284's plan; not built); (b) Application Support instead of caches; (c) keep the memory in the private iCloud database (R285); (d) analyse only on demand from the episode's menu; (e) a trained model (R286); (f) no on-device detection |

The screenshot job's generated sample clip (`UITestClip`, Debug builds only) drew a lighter band 48 pixels wide moving 8 pixels a frame across its 640-pixel frame from x 0, so the first frame (and every 80th) had it at x 0–23, which is x 0–72 at 1920: the strip in the pictures. Fix: every frame is one even colour (`SampleClipFrames`, AppLogic); the hue still changes every second, so a still is never black. Linux test `SampleClipFramesTests` (each frame of a second the same, a new hue every second, twelve hues, never black). (2) Apple's pages (read 21 Nov 2026): AVMetadataIdentifier lists `iTunesMetadataContentRating`, "An identifier that represents the content rating in iTunes" (tvOS 9.0+); the article "Customizing the tvOS playback experience" (linked from AVPlayerViewController) lists "Content rating \| iTunesMetadataContentRating \| String" among the metadata the player supports and says the player presents the values other than title and subtitle in its Info tab. AVPlayerViewController has no rating property of its own. So the rating is the system's item: `PlayerCoordinator` appends it to the item's external metadata after playback starts (like the poster), from the same layered details as the product page (R371: source, then TMDB, then Wikidata); an episode uses its show's rating (`PlayerRating.detailsKey`); no item without a rating or for "NR", "Not Rated". Now Playing: `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter`'s keys (read 21 Nov 2026) include no content rating, and the system owns the entry on tvOS (R317), so the app adds none. | R380 | **The content rating goes on the player item before playback starts (owner's provisional decision of 22 Nov 2026 under the rule that Apple's published guidance wins for functionality; follows R319).** WWDC19 session 503 (developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2019/503) says to provide the rating "at the start of playback" so AVKit can check the Apple TV's content restrictions. (1) Before the start: the rating already on hand with no request: Plex's `contentRating` from the listing its shared client last finished reading (`PlexClient.heldContentRating`, kept after the listing expires; never starts a request), else the rating stored in the library (`LibraryItem.contentRating`: the NFO file's or the server's single-item answer, R374). Jellyfin and Emby give none at this point: `SourceBuilder` makes a new client for each call, so no listing is held; their stored rating still counts. Nothing on hand, or only "NR", "Not Rated", "Unrated" or "" (none recorded): no rating item at start. (2) After the start, as in R319: the online lookup (the layered details: source, then TMDB, then Wikidata, R371) runs on its own; its answer replaces the item's rating only when the item has none or the answer's layer ranks at least as high (a TMDB or Wikidata answer never replaces a source rating; an answer of unknown layer ranks lowest); the old item is removed first, so there is never more than one rating item. The start never waits on any lookup. The rules are in AppLogic (`PlayerRating.atStart`, `PlayerRating.afterLookup`) with Linux tests in `PlayerRatingTests` (source rating, stored only, neither, "NR"/"Not Rated" ignored, a weaker rating replaced, a stronger one kept). Consequence: a title rated above the Apple TV's content restriction may be blocked at the start (a passcode prompt, or no playback under a device profile); that is the system's behaviour, not the app's. Noticed in passing, not changed: for an https Jellyfin or Emby source `SourceBuilder` wraps the client in `TrustGuardedSource`, so the R374 list rating (`PlexServerMetadata.contentRating`, a cast to the client type) may answer nil there (unverified). UNVERIFIED, first on the device run: whether the Info tab and the content restrictions accept a plain value such as "PG-13" (R319); then whether a rating set before the start reaches the restrictions, whether a replacement after the start is shown, and whether the held Plex listing is present at start in practice (the link step usually has just read it). | PROVISIONAL (owner, 22 Nov 2026). | (chosen) a rating on hand before the start, the online one after it when stronger; (a) after the start only, as in R319 (restrictions may not see it); (b) wait for the online lookup before starting (refused: the start never waits on a lookup); (c) also start a Jellyfin or Emby listing at start (a request; refused) |

| R384 | **Jellyfin's and Emby's own content rating also over https: the rating lookups look through the trust guard (owner's request after the cast audit of 22 Nov 2026).** Why: `SourceBuilder` wraps every https Jellyfin and Emby source in `TrustGuardedSource` (a pin is made for every https address, approved or not), so the four casts in `PlexServerMetadata` (`as? JellyfinClient`, `as? EmbyClient` in `contentRating(for:)` and `requestContentRating(for:)`, R374) failed on the guard, and the server's own rating was never used for those servers: the list rating answered nil and the single-item request threw "unsupported", so the rating came from the NFO file, TMDB or Wikidata only. Now `TrustGuardedSource.unwrapped(_:)` (VideoPlayerCore, next to the guard) returns the source inside any number of guards, and any other source as it is; only those four casts use it. The Plex casts and every other cast are unchanged (Plex is never wrapped). Linux tests in `TrustGuardedSourceTests`: a plain source stays itself, a wrapped one gives the inner one, a double wrap the innermost, another kind of wrapper stays itself. Trade-off: a call made through the unwrapped client skips the guard's step that turns a pin refusal into `TrustRefusedError`, so a refused certificate shows up here as the transport's vague error (both lookups already treat any error as "no answer now"); the pin itself still applies, because the pinned transport is inside the client, and a refusal is still reported to the trust list by the pin's own callback (`CertificatePin`, read, not run). UNVERIFIED: that the rating now arrives from a real Jellyfin or Emby server over https (needs a device run and a server). | Decided (owner's request). | (chosen) look through the guard for these four casts; (a) leave https Jellyfin/Emby servers on the NFO, TMDB and Wikidata layers (before R384); (b) give the guard itself the rating calls, keeping the refusal-to-`TrustRefusedError` step (not requested) |
| R385 | **Compile error on ca23e66: `LayeredDetails`' initializer was internal (GitHub compile check, owner's report of 22 Nov 2026).** `App/Support/OnlineServices.swift:182` (R380, `layeredDetails(key:)`) builds `LayeredDetails(details:used:)` in the app target, but the struct in AppLogic had only Swift's memberwise initializer, which is internal, so the compiler refused it across the module boundary ("initializer is inaccessible due to 'internal' protection level"). Fix: an explicit `public init(details: TitleDetails? = nil, used: [DetailsLayer], ratingLayer: DetailsLayer? = nil)`; the type and its three stored properties were already public. The other uses in the app (`PlayerCoordinator` reads `ratingLayer`, `OnlineServices` reads `details`) read public properties only. **Why it was not caught before:** the call sits inside the `#if DEBUG` UI-test fixtures block, so a Release build does not compile it; `scripts/parse-app.sh` checks syntax only, not access levels; and the AppLogic package tests use `@testable import AppLogic`, which can reach internal members. New test `LayeredDetailsPublicInitTests` sits in a file of SourcePlexTests that imports AppLogic without `@testable`, as the app does, so it only compiles while the initializer is public. **UNVERIFIED:** that the app now compiles (only the owner's next compile check can show it, R318). | Done | (a) A public initializer in AppLogic (chosen). (b) Build the value in AppLogic with a public factory and call that from the app. |

| R386 | **Playback ends about 30 seconds in (owner's device test of 22 Nov 2026): one cause fixed, the rest asked; the failure screen names the player's error and its last event.** Everything in the code that can end a playback near 30 s, read on 22 Nov: (1) the start's limits, `PlaybackStartDeadline` (AppLogic/PlaybackStartLog.swift): 30 s for the item to become ready, 30 s from `play()` to playing, 20 s for the resume seek, 60 s for the resolution, checked once a second (PlayerCoordinator `everySecond`, PlayerScreen `PlaybackStartWatch`); (2) the item's status `.failed` (PlayerCoordinator `itemStatusChanged`); (3) the app's own source reads, 30 s request timeouts (App/Support/PinnedTransport.swift, VideoPlayerCore HTTPTransport, SMBBackend), which reach the player only in converted playback (the loopback answers an error when a read fails); (4) nothing else: no buffer setting (`preferredForwardBufferDuration`, `automaticallyWaitsToMinimizeStalling` keep the system defaults), no link or token lifetime handled by the app, no server transcode session (the servers are never asked to transcode), the R309 timeline only reports, the Up Next and skip logic only seek or start the next episode, and there is no other watchdog. **Fixed (provable from the code):** the start left "Starting playback" only on a CHANGE of the play state seen after `play()`; a player already playing by then (Play pressed on the remote during the move to the saved position) sends none, so the 30-second limit ended a video that was playing, with "The video did not start". Now the start also counts as playing when the player says so or the playhead moved 0.5 s past where play was asked for, checked right after `play()`, on every playhead tick and on the one-second clock (`PlaybackStartProgress`, Linux tests). Whether this was the owner's case is UNVERIFIED. **Asked (suspected only, behaviour unchanged):** (a) a stall in direct play: Apple's playbackStalledNotification page says "File-based playback doesn't continue", and the app did not observe stalls; (b) a source read that waits 30 s in converted playback (the transports' request timeout) ending the item; (c) the R316 on-device learning opening a second reader of the same file over the network right after play. Each is now recorded as an event (stalls, failed-to-play-to-end with its error, error-log entries, play states with the system's waiting reason, item status, time jumps), and the failure screen and Settings > Playback show the player's error domain, code, message and underlying errors and the last event with the playhead, the seconds since the player opened and the clock time (`PlayerErrorReport`, `PlaybackEvent`, `PlaybackEventLog`, Linux tests). The common behaviour of other tvOS players, described generically from general knowledge (not checked, no outside contact): a stall shows the buffering indicator and waits, reads are retried, and an error is shown only after retries fail, never after a fixed time once playback runs. | PROVISIONAL (the fix is decided by the code; the questions are open). | (chosen) fix the start's playing check, record events, show the error domain, code, underlying errors and last event; (a) also end playback with a message on a stall in direct play (changes behaviour; waits for the next report); (b) raise or remove the 30 s request timeout of source reads in converted playback (waits for the next report); (c) start the R316 learning only after the first minutes of playback, or not on network sources (waits for the next report); (d) no change, report only |

| R390 | **An episode never became ready to play: black player, about 30 s, "timed out" (owner's device test of 22 Nov 2026). Cause NOT proven; start checkpoints added so the next failure says where it stopped.** Which limit: the player's own steps are timed by the coordinator's one-second clock (`PlayerCoordinator.everySecond`, App/Features/Player/PlayerCoordinator.swift:384) against `PlaybackStartDeadline` (AppLogic/PlaybackStartLog.swift:49-55). The 60 s resolution limit (line 49) cannot be it: it runs while the loading view is up (`PlaybackStartWatch`, PlayerScreen.swift:120), not over the black player. An item that never becomes ready ends at the 30 s ready limit (line 51), counted from `tracker.enter(.playerReady)` (PlayerCoordinator.swift:171); the screen then says "This step took longer than 30 seconds: waiting for the system player to open the file." and "Step: Waiting for the system player to open the file", with no Error line unless the item or its error log had one. The same black screen also follows an item that became ready but never played (30 s after `play()`, line 55, "Step: Starting playback") and, for a resumed episode, a resume seek that never finished (20 s, line 53, "Step: Moving to the saved position"); only the Step line tells them apart, which is why the checkpoints below were added. Order of the start: resolve (find file, check format, ask Plex `plexConversionNeeded` AppServices.swift:463, open the converter :477 or get the link :488, read segments :503 and the held rating :513, all under the 60 s limit); then the screen hands the result over (PlayerScreen.swift:135); the coordinator creates the asset and item (PlayerCoordinator.swift:107) after all of that; then ready; then the resume seek (:351), `play()`, and only then the artwork and rating tasks (:363, :365), `probe.load()` (:366) and R316 learning (:369). So nothing in the app's own start work runs between the item's creation and its readiness except the observers. R316 (EpisodeAudioReader.swift, its own `AVURLAsset` and `AVAssetReader`) is called only from `SegmentService.learn` (SegmentService.swift:52), which only `startLearning()` calls, after ready and `play()`: it cannot hold back readiness, so it is NOT deferred (the owner's rule: defer only if the evidence points to it). What can still keep the item from ready for 30 s, unproven: (1) converted playback: AVPlayer must fetch master.m3u8, video.m3u8, init_video.mp4 and the first video and audio segments from the loopback (RemuxEngine/Session.swift `resource`), each built on demand from source reads with a 30 s request timeout (App/Support/PinnedTransport.swift:18, VideoPlayerCore/HTTP/HTTPTransport.swift:43-45, SourceSMB/SMBBackend.swift:26); a slow source, a read that waits out that timeout, or a slow DTS/TrueHD to FLAC conversion of the first audio segment would leave the item unready; a handler error answers 500 (Loopback.swift `runHandler`). (2) direct playback: the server's answer to AVPlayer's own requests, the `AVURLAssetHTTPHeaderFieldsKey` header option (not a documented constant), a file whose index sits at its end, or an https server whose certificate only the app's pinned transport trusts (AVPlayer does not use that pin). (3) a video track the Apple TV cannot decode (codec, profile, Dolby Vision, container). Added (diagnostics only, no limit changed): `PlaybackCheckpoints` (AppLogic/PlaybackCheckpoints.swift), timed from the start by the tracker: File Opened (direct: link known; converted: header read), Loopback Is Ready (converted only), Item Created, Item Is Ready to Play, Video Track Found (codec FourCC and size, with the system's `isDecodable`/`isPlayable` in plain words; converted: the engine's codec), First Frame Presented (`AVPlayerViewController.isReadyForDisplay`, Apple: "A Boolean value that indicates whether the player item's first video frame is ready for display. This property is key-value observable."), Audio Started (an enabled audio track while the playhead moves; the player does not report sound reaching the TV), Playhead at +5 s and +30 s (moved that far past where play was asked for); the path (Direct, Converted, Sample Clip); a Start Result of "never ready", "ready, no picture" or "a picture, then stopped"; notes for a ready item with no video track; and in converted playback `LoopbackServeLog` (RemuxEngine) with the player's first requests, when each was answered or failed, and the start resources never asked for. They show on the failure screen, in Settings > Playback > Last Playback Problem and in the Full Problem Report, redacted like the other fields; problems stored earlier still decode. STILL UNKNOWN: which path the owner's episode took (container, so direct or converted); whether the item reached ready (the owner's "never ready" is consistent with the screen but not shown by it); whether the loopback was asked for anything and answered; whether the video track is one the Apple TV decodes. UNVERIFIED: nothing here has run on a device or been compiled for tvOS (Linux tests and the syntax check only); that `isReadyForDisplay` KVO fires for this app's AVPlayerViewController; that `loadTracks` answers before the item is ready for a remote file. | PROVISIONAL (diagnostics; the cause waits for the next report) | (chosen) checkpoints and the loopback log, limits unchanged; (a) raise the 30 s ready limit (refused by the owner: it hides the symptom); (b) defer R316 until the playhead has moved 30 s (only if a report shows it competing; the code shows it starts after ready); (c) try direct playback when converted playback has served nothing in N s, or the reverse (changes playback, needs the owner); (d) log every loopback request, not only the start's (larger report) |

| R399 | **The tvOS compile check of 406ad8c failed: "App/Shared/GridToolbar.swift:102:43: error: cannot find 'ProductPageSpacing' in scope".** What broke: R392 (in 3673708) made `GridCountLine` use AppLogic's `ProductPageSpacing.aboveFirstGridRow`, and GridToolbar.swift imported SwiftUI only. Fixed: `import AppLogic` added. Why the Linux checks missed it: `scripts/parse-app.sh` is `swiftc -parse`, syntax only (its own header: it cannot catch unknown names or missing imports); the Linux tests build the packages, never the app target; design-lint and pre-push-check do not look at names. Checked by hand for the 24 files under App/ and UITests/ touched by 3673708, d400129, cf7e523, 37ea2dc, d2cf7d0 and 406ad8c: every package type each one names (AppLogic, PlaybackStatus, PlaybackLogic, VideoPlayerCore, LibraryIndex, MetadataOnline, UserData) is imported, GridToolbar.swift was the only file missing one; UITests/ScreenshotTests.swift needs no import, since the UI test target compiles the ScreenChecks sources and App/Shared/ControlID.swift itself (project.yml). The app-target names the new code calls (`services.openRequest`, `Route.series`, `ReadableSecondary.style`, `ControlID.featured`, `ControlID.clearRecentSearches`, `RecentSearches.heading`/`clearTitle`, `DetailPageLayout.belowActions`, `ProductPageLayout.heroFraction`, `SymbolSlot.body`, `FittingLine`, `RatingBadge`, `BadgeRow(badges:separate:)`, `card.subtitleLine`, `card.formatBadges`, `navigationChrome(in:)`, `usesSidebar(_:)`, `focusContent(in:)`) exist with the labels used. What now catches it: ScreenChecksTests `testEveryAppFileThatUsesAnAppLogicTypeImportsAppLogic` (SharedFaultsTests.swift) reads every file under App/ and fails when one names a public AppLogic type (the spacing enums of VisualSpacing.swift and every other) without `import AppLogic`, leaving out names the app declares itself, nested AppLogic types and text inside string literals. `testTheMissingImportOf406ad8cIsCaught` proves it on a copy of GridToolbar.swift as it was; and with the import taken out of the real file for one run, the first test failed with "App/Shared/GridToolbar.swift uses ProductPageSpacing without import AppLogic" (the import was put back). Still NOT caught on Linux: a missing import of the other packages, a wrong label or type, anything else only the tvOS compiler sees. | Decided (owner's request of 22 Nov 2026: the import and a Linux check; the test rather than parse-app.sh was this session's pick, "whichever fits") | (chosen) a ScreenChecks test over all AppLogic types; (a) the same check in scripts/parse-app.sh (bash, no Swift test build); (b) extend it to every package module the app imports |
| R400 | **Task covers flat black (owner's review of the run on f0a67d3, item 1: 10-add-source-types, 11 to 19, 20, 21, 93, 94, 99, 100, 110, 111).** Cause, from the code: R397 put the regular material on the cover's ROOT (`TaskCoverChrome`, App/Shared/SettingsPane.swift), which is OUTSIDE the `NavigationStack` every cover holds (SourceSetupFlow.swift:17, FixMatchView.swift:17, SaveViewSheet.swift:12, SortMoveSheet.swift:42, FileDetailsSheet.swift:15). The stack and its pages are above that root; the run shows no change at all, which fits an opaque layer above the material: the stack's own container or the `Form`'s scroll background. Which of the two it is is not on an Apple page read (UNVERIFIED); `containerBackground(_:for: .navigation)` would put a background inside the stack, but the page of its `.navigation` placement lists iOS, iPadOS, Mac Catalyst and watchOS, no tvOS (the `containerBackground(_:for:)` modifier itself lists tvOS 17.0+; corrected 22 Nov 2026, the earlier text said the modifier's page). Fix: every page a cover shows draws the grey itself, inside the stack (`TaskCoverPageBackground`: the regular material over the opaque system background, full bleed), only when `isInTaskCover`. **Corrected by R412:** this first also hid the `Form`'s scroll background with `scrollContentBackground(.hidden)`, which does not exist on tvOS (compile check run #26: "'scrollContentBackground' is unavailable in tvOS"; Apple's page lists iOS, iPadOS, Mac Catalyst, macOS, visionOS and watchOS); it is removed. Whether a tvOS `Form` draws an opaque background of its own is on no Apple page read: UNVERIFIED, the next run's pictures decide. Pages that take it: TaskPage (Add a Source, the forms, Your Network, File Details, Save View, Fix Match), SettingsPane (How This Was Sorted, Move), TextEntryPage, LongTextPage. That this grey equals the Settings and Files pages' grey is UNVERIFIED: what draws that grey on a tab is not in the app's code (no background of ours there), so it is the system's, and the material over black may be darker. | R414 | **Task covers still flat black (run #27: all 26 -COVER notes "cover background #000000"; R397, R400 and R412 did not change it).** Cause, from Apple's `Material` page (tvOS 15.0+, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/material): a material is "like inserting a translucent layer between the modified view and its background" and blurs "a background that's part of your app". R397 and R400 put `.regularMaterial` directly on `BackgroundStyle()` (black in the dark appearance), so it had only black to blur. Nothing draws black over it: at the four corners the page's own background is the top layer (TaskPage's `Form` is a centred column, 2/3 of the width, `TaskPageLayout.columnFraction`), so neither the full-screen presentation, the navigation stack's container nor the `Form` is the cause; that also fits R397 (outside the stack) and R400 (inside it) giving exactly the same black. The tabs are grey because something of the system's sidebar-adaptable tab view draws there (the app draws no background on Settings or Files); a full-screen cover is presented outside the tab view. Which layer of the tab view: UNVERIFIED. Fix: `TaskCoverFill` (App/Shared/SettingsPane.swift), used by the cover's root and by every cover page: the material, over `BackgroundStyle().secondary` (`secondary`, "Returns the second level of this shape style", tvOS 17.0+, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/shapestyle/secondary-swift.property; `BackgroundStyle`, tvOS 14.0+, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/backgroundstyle), over the plain opaque `BackgroundStyle()`, so the cover stays opaque. System styles only. UNVERIFIED: what the second level of the background looks like on tvOS (no Apple page shows it); if run #28 still measures #000000, it is black there too and the next step is one of the alternatives. `UIColor.secondarySystemBackground` lists no tvOS (iOS, iPadOS, Mac Catalyst, visionOS: https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/uicolor/secondarysystembackground) and is now in TvOSAvailabilityTests. `presentationBackground(_:)` lists tvOS 16.4+ but speaks of "the enclosing sheet" and lets views behind show through translucent styles (R362), so it is not used. | PROVISIONAL; CONFIRMED by run #28 on 4af6cb0 (all 29 -COVER notes: background #161618, no COVER PROBLEM) | (a) The material over the background's second level over the opaque background (chosen). (b) No opaque base: the material over the page behind, a frosted cover (the page shows through blurred; R362 and pixel check B 1 would need changing). (c) `.thickMaterial` or `.ultraThickMaterial` over (b). (d) Keep flat black (the system's own background for a full-screen presentation). |

| R424 | **Up climbs back up the product pages (run #28 on 4af6cb0, item 1).** 58-movie-detail-SCROLL-UP: Up from "Sample Actor 4, Sample Role 4" (x 755) stayed there four times, so 59-fix-match was not reached; 56-show-page-SCROLL-UP: Up from "S2, E3, A very long episode title" (x 980) stayed four times. Cause, as in R413: the focus engine looks for a focusable view in the direction of travel, and the buttons, the season picker and the first cards of a row sit at the left, so from a card at the right nothing was above. Fix: the hero (movie, episode and show page) is a `focusSection()` the page's whole width with `defaultFocus($playFocused, true, priority: .userInitiated)`, so Up from any entry below lands in it on Play or Resume; the season tabs, the episode row (show page and the episode page's other episodes) and the cast row are each a full-width focus section (`productPageRow()`, ProductPageParts.swift). Apple's `focusSection()` page: "the view's frame becomes capable of accepting focus in order to direct it at its nearest focusable descendant in the direction of travel"; `defaultFocus` page: priority `userInitiated` uses the preferred focus "in response to user-driven focus navigation as well". Whether the hero hands the focus to Play rather than More on tvOS 27 is UNVERIFIED until the next run (56 and 58). Down from Resume reading "PG-13" (324, 158): not the rating badge (the metadata line has no focusable part); it is the Information block, one `.focusable()` block whose first row is Rating, and XCTest reported the block by its first value text because the block was no accessibility element of its own (UNVERIFIED how XCTest chose that text). The block is now one accessibility container, "Information", identifier `product.information`, rows still read one by one. Tests: ReviewRun28Tests. | PROVISIONAL | (a) Full-width focus sections per row and a hero that hands the focus to Play (chosen). (b) Focus sections only, no `defaultFocus`: Up lands on the nearest hero button (often More). (c) Move every row's first focusable item to the left edge: no help for a card at the right. (d) Leave it: a person moves left first. |

| R426 | **PlaybackStartWatch: a real race between the limit and a cancelled step (run #28, item 3).** tvOS Simulator only: "testAValueThatArrivesAfterTheLimitGoesToOnLate : failed - finished(7)". When a step passed its limit, the watch cancelled the step FIRST and decided the outcome after; a step that ends on cancellation (as most network work does) could return in between, and its value won as `.finished` although the limit had passed, so the start would go on with a value the cancelled step produced instead of reporting the overdue step (the device playback timeout of R406). Fix: the overdue outcome is decided first (`OutcomeOnce.resume`), then the step is cancelled; the late value goes to `onLate`, where the caller closes what it opened. The test is not loosened; a new test runs the race 200 times with no yield between the cancellation and the return. Whether that test failed on Linux before the fix was not tried (it passed there before). | PROVISIONAL | (a) Decide, then cancel (chosen). (b) Keep the order and drop a value that arrives after cancellation: the work would still be reported as finished when it returns first. (c) A lock around cancel and resume: more code for the same order. |

| R441 | **Plex MKV episodes fail with HTTP 500 from our loopback (owner's device test of 23 Nov 2026; R390 continued): diagnostics only, no behaviour change.** Evidence (Settings > Playback > Last Playback Problem, Plex MKV, every episode, however small): Path Converted; MKV; avc1.640028, ec-3; File Opened 0.3 s; Item Created 1.0 s; Item Ready, Video Track, First Frame, Audio Started not reached; error CoreMediaErrorDomain -16847, HTTP 500, 25 s after the player opened. Read in the code: `prepare()` runs in `RemuxPlayback.open` BEFORE the server starts and before the item exists (File Opened 0.3 s is the end of `prepare()`), so the 500 is NOT from the first `prepare()`: `resource(path:)` calls `prepare()` again and gets the stored result. A 500 comes only from a handler that throws (`Loopback.runHandler` turns any error into `.failed`, then 500, body "internal error"); the likeliest throwers are the segment requests (`seg_video_k`, `seg_audio_n_k`) that read a window through the Plex source. The Plex source throws `rangesNotSupported` when a range read is answered 200 (PlexClient.read), `httpStatus` for any other status, and the transport's own error after its 30 s request timeout (App/Support/PinnedTransport.swift:18; URLSessionTransport default 30 s, HTTPTransport.swift:45). Whether the 25 s is such a timeout, a Plex answer or the player's own retries is UNVERIFIED. Cost of a file without Cues (measured by test, 2400 clusters, 0.3 ms per read): one RAW 4 KB read per cluster header (`MatroskaClusterScanner.scan` reads through the session's reader, not through its own block cache, which is created and unused), so reads = clusters + 1 to 2; for 1 GB with about 4000 clusters: about 4,010 reads in `prepare()` = 160 s at 40 ms, 320 s at 80 ms, 600 s at 150 ms, all before the player is started. A file WITH Cues needs about 3 to 8 reads (128 KB blocks) plus the first window. Now recorded in the Full Problem Report: every phase of `prepare()` (index load, cues or cluster scan, planning, first window, track set-up) with seconds, reads and bytes, then the total; and a loopback entry for a request that threw reads `answered 500 after X s: <error text>` with the error redacted (token and key values, one line, 300 characters). Tests: RemuxEngineTests/PrepareDiagnosticsTests. | Diagnostics done; CAUSE UNPROVEN until the next device run prints the 500 text | (a) Nothing more until the report shows the text. (b) Log every loopback request, not only the first ones. |
| R442 | **Plex start: proposals NOT implemented (follow-up of R441).** (1) Start the player item before `prepare()` ends for files without Cues, with a progress state ("Reading the file's index") instead of a black screen; (2) read cluster headers in larger blocks or several at once: the scanner's own `BlockCachedReader` is created and never used, but clusters of 1 GB / 4000 = about 250 KB are further apart than any block, so a block cache alone gains nothing there; what helps is reading several headers in parallel (for example 8 at a time: 4000 reads at 80 ms = 320 s become about 40 s), or finding clusters from the Cues/SeekHead of a remuxed copy; (3) if the next report shows `rangesNotSupported` (200 to a range request), add the Plex transcoder-free Part URL as a direct play path instead of the remux, or ask the user. | PROVISIONAL: no behaviour changes until R441's report names the failing request | (a) Do (1) only. (b) Do (2) only (parallel header reads). (c) Both. (d) Stream the file by one long range request instead of many small ones. |

| R447 | **Card titles never cut mid-letter (item 6).** Both cards already use `FittingText(style: .caption)`: one line, shrunk to the readable floor, then the marquee at the floor (R229), never a cut. | R449 | **Metadata edits: a user layer over every source (owner's request of 23 Nov 2026, commit 3).** New record type `edit` in the user data (`UserData/Edits.swift`): one record per item, one stamped field per edited value (`f.title`, `f.overview`, ...), `null` = reset, `schema` = format 1. The newest edit of each field wins on any device; export and CloudKit sync carry it with the other records (existing 1 MB record limit; text only, so far below it). Read precedence: user, then local sidecar, online data, embedded tags (`MetadataMerger.merge(user:)`, `FieldSource.user`; the earlier order B3 below the user layer is unchanged). A rescan, Fix Match, an online refresh or a Plex re-index writes other records and caches and never an edit record (tests: EditOverlayTests). Fields: title, sort title, original title, tagline, description, release date, year, content rating, studio or network, runtime, season and episode numbers, episode title and air date, genres, collections, labels, cast, crew (name and role), and artwork: poster, background, logo, season poster, episode still. Reset per field (a row on each field's page) and per item (Reset All Edits, the one confirmation). Edits stay local to the app and its iCloud data; nothing is written back to a server or to files. Screens: Edit in the detail page's More menu and in the card's long-press menu, a hub with General, Artwork, Cast and Crew, Tags; Save and Cancel are rows of each page (the page hides the navigation bar, rule D1); a long description is `TextField(axis: .vertical)` (tvOS 16.0+; `TextEditor` has no tvOS version, Apple's page). NOT DONE: the Edit item on show and season pages; the edited title, sort title and artwork on cards, grids and sorting (the overlay is read by the movie and episode page's description, cast and content rating only); the pickers for online candidates and pictures beside the file (a typed web address works); a picture from an iPhone (tvOS cannot receive one without an app or AirDrop on the phone's side; not built). UNVERIFIED: every screen on a device, `TextField(axis:)` in a Form on tvOS 27. | PROVISIONAL: local edits with their own record type. | (a) Write the edits back to Plex (its API accepts field edits; needs a locked-field decision and write rights). (b) Write an NFO file beside each video (read-only sources cannot take it). (c) Keep the edits inside the library database (lost with the database; rejected: R163). |

An item known under several keys reads them all and the newest stamp wins per field (`UserDataLedger.edits(for:)`). A moved or renamed file with no online id loses its file key; `rekeyEdits(from:to:)` carries the edits over when the scan knows it is the same file (nothing calls it yet: the scan does not detect a move). | R457 | **Fixes to R456 after the owner's review of its diff (23 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL), commit A of three.** (1) SYNC ORDER: a setting is written to the user data only when THIS device changed it since it last wrote or read it (a baseline per key, kept on this device under vp.prefs.baseline, never synced). After every completed sync (CloudSyncHost.onSynced), at launch and after a restore the device first writes its own pending edits, then takes the newest values of the record. Opening Settings only writes edits, so a newer value from another device is never overwritten by this device's older stored value. A device with no baseline treats what it stores now as already synced (nothing old is written with a new stamp). Test: device A launches, B's newer edit arrives, A opens Settings, B's value is still the newest. (2) EVERY SETTING SYNCS, one field each, newest per field, schema 1, a newer schema is not read, a wrong type is ignored, an integer stays an integer: sortOrder, hideSpoilers, showContinueWatching, showNextUp, showRecentlyAdded, continueWatchingDays, showDiscover, scanOnLaunch, showTitleLogos, preferLocalArtwork, onlineRegion, onlineFreeSources, metadataLanguage, artworkLanguage, streamingCache, autoPlayNextEpisode, showSkipButtons, skipIntrosAutomatically, skipCreditsAutomatically, showPlaybackStatsButton, rememberTrackChoicePerShow, trueHDCompatibilityCore (22). NEVER: online keys and tokens, passwords, the Parental PIN, source addresses and account names (their own sync), and the device-only values: recent searches, the four backup switches and the last-backup time (each device backs up its own library), the device ID, the playback problem report, the baseline. Accessibility has no stored setting of its own (it shows the system's). CORRECTION of R456: its footer said passwords are never synced or backed up; that was wrong (source passwords have their own encrypted iCloud copy, CredentialStorageText). The footer now says only what is true of the settings record. (3) SYNC AND BACKUP is ONE page (the hub and the two pages are gone; BackupSettingsView and StorageSettingsView removed, RestoreBanner kept): iCloud status and Sync Now, what is backed up, the sizes, Last Backup, Back Up Now, Restore from iCloud, Passwords note. Titles, so ControlIDs, unchanged. Pictures: 37-settings-storage-and-icloud dropped (same screen as 37-settings-sync-and-backup); the Backup accessibility pictures now open the one page. (4) PREFER LOCAL ARTWORK now covers movie posters (R456), show posters, backgrounds of movies and shows and logos of movies and shows, each with the user's own choice first; off = online, then the file, then the server. A season has no picture of its own in the engine (it uses the show's poster) and an episode's online 'poster' is its show's (R387), so episode pictures and stills stay their own file first in both orders: there is no online still to reorder. The footer says so. (5) HIDE SPOILERS also covers the episode page's picture and description, the Continue Watching and Next Up cards (a started episode counts as not watched: PROVISIONAL, alternative: started episodes show) and the player's Info tab (description replaced, no picture of the episode set); one rule, SpoilerRules; the footer lists the places. (6) the literal key is AppPreference.hideSpoilers.rawValue. (7) Larger Text: 118-larger-text-settings-playback (and its default-size partner 119-default-settings-playback, which scripts/ci-a11y-compare.py needs); the top level is 82. PICTURES AND TIME: plan 216 (was 215): -1 in part 1 (about -20 s) and +2 in the accessibility job (about +40 s), at the 19.8 s per picture of R232 (an ESTIMATE, not measured). CI_TEST_BUDGET_SECONDS read from .github/workflows/compile-check.yml: 2400 for Screenshots part 1 and part 2, 1800 for part 3, 1500 for the accessibility job; its tests were about 1092 s on the run of 20 Nov (test06 751 + test11 341), so about 1130 s of 1500 with this commit; unverified until a run. UNPROVEN until a picture is seen: the one Sync and Backup page, the blur on the cards and the episode page, Info tab replacement on a device. | PROVISIONAL: the baseline rule, the 22 keys, started episodes hidden. | (a) Push everything on every sync (rejected: the bug). (b) Sync the four backup switches too. (c) Started episodes show. (d) Keep the two Sync and Backup pages under a hub. |
| R458 | **Settings left out of R456, commit B of three (23 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL).** (1) LANGUAGE AND SUBTITLES: done. Language > Audio and > Subtitles (Auto = the Apple TV's language, plus 11 common languages), Playback > Subtitles (Off, Forced Only, Auto, Always On). The player applies them before play through `AVPlayerItem.select(_:in:)` (Apple: it selects one option of a group and deselects the rest). A show's own remembered choice (stored on this device, `vp.trackChoice.<show>`) always wins; with all three on Auto the system's own choice stands, so nothing changes for a person who never opens them. Tested at package level with synthetic tracks (`TrackDefaults`). (2) SKIP SECONDS: not built. Apple's `AVPlayerViewControllerSkippingBehavior` page: `.default` skips in 10-second intervals, `.skipItem` skips to the next or previous item; no interval setting exists. (3) PLAYBACK: done. Resume Playback (default on; off starts at 0, Start Over unchanged); Continuous Playback (the old Play Next Episode Automatically, same stored value); Inactivity Check (Off, After 2, 3 or 4 Episodes, default Off; "Are you still watching?" pauses until Select; only episodes that advanced on their own count; a Play Now press at the Up Next card cannot be told apart and counts as automatic, UNVERIFIED); Playback Speed through `AVPlayer.defaultRate`. Live Previews and the Dolby Vision switch stay out: the system decides. (4) AUDIO PAGE: built with one row that works, Volume Boost (Off, Low +3 dB, High +6 dB). It applies only to audio the app decodes itself (DTS, TrueHD, Opus, Vorbis; hard limit, loud passages can clip). Apple's `AVMutableAudioMixInputParameters.setVolume(_:at:)` accepts 0.0 to 1.0 only, so system-decoded audio cannot be raised. Downmix Volume and LFE in Stereo Downmix stay out (the system downmixes; the app has no mixing stage). One line points Dolby Atmos to the Apple TV's own Settings. (5) LIBRARY AND SOURCE: done. Library: Refresh Metadata (all sources, with a confirmation; disabled without Online Lookup, R209), Last Scan, Titles, Shows, Episodes and Files counts. Source: Hide (one query-layer rule, `LibraryRestrictions`; a title also held by a shown source stays), Edit (the name), Forget (red; the confirmation says what is removed and that the server is untouched), Refresh Metadata for one source, Speed Test (reads 8 MB of one file, shows Mbps, read-only; PROVISIONAL, alternative: none). (6) COLLECTIONS AND GROUPS: the model holds a user-edited Collections label per title (Edit > Collections) and nothing from the online source: a film's collection is one request per film (`DiscoverEngine.collectionGaps` asks, caches nothing per title). Group Movies Into Collections is NOT built in this commit: it needs a stored collection link per film and a grouped Movies screen, and neither was written or seen; it is listed as the next item, not dropped. Select Version and Play Version (Auto, Highest Quality, Most Recent, Ask) are built on the existing versions menu: Auto plays the first file the scan found; a version chosen on the page always wins; Ask asks on the title's page and stores the answer as Select Version, other places play Auto. Custom Collections and Smart Groups: proposal only. A Custom Collection would be a named, user-ordered list stored in the user data and synced; a Smart Group a saved filter (kinds, genres, years, rating, watch state, resolution) evaluated by the existing query layer. Nothing is built. (7) METADATA AND ARTWORK: Prefer Local Metadata done (default on = today's order: source, then Wikidata, then TMDB; off = online before the files; the rating follows the same order; your own edits always first; every combination tested). Prefer Embedded Artwork not built: the Matroska reader gives an attachment's name and size, never its data or position. Show External Ratings and Rating Sources stay out until a source supplies a score (R453). (8) GENERAL: done. Show Poster Titles (default on), Watched Indicators (check and progress on cards; default on), Show Filenames (default off), List View (default off; rows of a small poster, title and lines, from system rows). Auto-Select Next and Up Next List stay out: no meaning was stated. (9) PAGE RULES: flat rows, value at the right, pickers for more than two values, at most one short line under a row. All 13 new settings join the sync (35 keys): audioLanguage, subtitleLanguage, subtitleMode, resumePlayback, playbackSpeed, inactivityCheck, playVersion, volumeBoost, showPosterTitles, watchedIndicators, showFilenames, listView, preferLocalMetadata. One new picture, 46-settings-audio (test03, about 19.8 s by R232's estimate; part 1 has 2400 s). Not compiled for tvOS: the screens are syntax-checked only, so every new page, List View and the Inactivity prompt stay unproven until a picture or device run is seen. | PROVISIONAL: Volume Boost only on app-decoded audio; Hide is hidden, not locked; Ask asks once on the page; Speed Test; Inactivity Check counts automatic advances; Group Movies Into Collections not built; Custom Collections and Smart Groups proposal only. | (a) Hide shows a lock instead (R459 decides for ratings). (b) Volume Boost through a system audio mix (rejected: 0.0 to 1.0 only). (c) Ask on every Play press, not once per title. (d) Group by a stored collection id fetched at scan time. (e) A skip interval through custom buttons instead of the system's 10 seconds. | |
| R459 | **Parental Controls, commit C of three (23 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NEVER COMPILED for tvOS).** APPLE PAGES READ: Keychain services; `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` (readable only while the device is unlocked; the item does not move to another device or come back from another device's backup); HIG Entering data (hide secret entry; tvOS: `TVDigitEntryViewController.isSecureDigitEntry`). WHAT tvOS GIVES AN APP for a passcode: the Keychain for a secret and the system's digit entry view. WHAT IT DOES NOT: no API reads or checks the Apple TV's own Restrictions passcode or its content limits, and no page read says a Keychain item survives deleting the app (UNVERIFIED), so the app keeps its own passcode and the report below does not promise a reset by reinstalling. THE RULE: one rule in the library query layer (`LibraryRestrictions.RatingRule`), applied inside page, count, letter index, one title by id, Continue Watching, Next Up, Recently Added, a show's episodes and the counts; tested on every one (`ParentalRatingTests`). A film follows its own rating; an episode with none takes the rating of another episode of its show (a show's information file names it once); a rating is read as capitals without spaces, hyphens, a country prefix or `Rated` (PG-13, Rated PG-13 and US:PG-13 agree). A rating in no allowed list is hidden. Allow Unrated (default OFF) decides the titles with none. The scales: US (G to NC-17; TV-Y to TV-MA), GB, DE; any other region uses the US one; defaults PG-13 and TV-14 (GB and DE 12). COVERAGE of the sites that do not use the rule: Search and Home read the same queries; Top Shelf is built from Continue Watching and Next Up and its pictures of titles no longer shown are deleted, and it is written again when a setting changes; the player's Episodes tab reads a show's episodes; Discover and online suggestions do not go through the library, so they are OFF while Restrictions are on; the Files tab and Needs Review list files by name, which the rule does not cover, so both are LEFT OUT while Restrictions are on. No query site is left open that a search of the app found (every use of the store's list, count, item and children calls was read); a site found later is a defect. WHAT DECIDES A TITLE'S RATING: only what the library keeps (the server's rating or the title's information file, read once). Most titles have none until read, so with Allow Unrated off a new library looks nearly empty: Read Ratings (on the page) reads every title's once; titles whose server and file give none stay hidden. Online ratings are not used (they are not stored per title). THE PASSCODE: four digits in the Keychain item `com.liamp.videoplayer.parental`, `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`, not synchronisable; the record is a random 16-byte salt, 10 000 rounds of SHA-256 and the lockout count, never the digits, never in UserDefaults, the user data, the sync or the backup (`ParentalControlsTests` runs the real settings sync and the user-data export with a passcode set and finds neither the keys nor the hash). The switches (`vp.restrict.*`) are on this device only, so another Apple TV is not locked by a passcode it does not have. Five wrong tries lock entry for 5 minutes, the lock is kept in the record (a restart does not clear it), and a locked entry is not compared. A set passcode is asked for before the page opens. NOT BUILT: Search Lock and Require PIN (Every Session, Every Time, Never for Allowed Titles): a restricted title is hidden, not locked, so there is no locked title to open or play for them to govern; Search already shows only allowed titles. FORGOTTEN PASSCODE (no outside contact, no recovery code): PROPOSAL, nothing built: (a) none, the person turns Restrictions off by removing the app's data in Settings (UNVERIFIED that the Keychain item goes with it); (b) a reset after a long wait (a day) that needs a Select press on the Apple TV, with no code; (c) ask the Apple TV's own Restrictions passcode (not possible, no API). UNPROVEN until a picture or device run: the digit entry view (47 and 48 show the page and the entry), the Files tab disappearing, Discover off, List and Top Shelf results, and the Keychain calls. | PROVISIONAL: hidden, not locked; Allow Unrated off; the default tiers; 5 tries and 5 minutes; Search Lock and Require PIN not built; episode takes the show's rating; Files and Needs Review left out while on; the passcode asked on opening the page. | (a) Titles shown locked with a lock, the PIN asked to open or play, and Require PIN with its three values (needs a locked state in every list and every play path). (b) Allow Unrated on. (c) Search behind the passcode. (d) The passcode in the user data (rejected: it would travel). (e) Another lockout (10 minutes, doubling). | |
