| R466 | **TestFlight build 202611242205 (the first built by the GitHub workflow, commit aca3d0b) crashes at launch on Apple TV 4K (AppleTV14,1), tvOS 27.3 beta (24K5298d) (owner's report, 24 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, CAUSE UNPROVEN).** (Moved to the exported .ipa: see R468.) FACTS (from the owner's message; the .ips file itself was not in Downloads when this was written, so nothing below was read from it): EXC_BREAKPOINT / SIGTRAP on the main thread about 0.4 s after launch; the top four frames are in CloudKit, then VideoPlayer frames at image offsets 45568, 45692, 45724, 16424, 44972, 9419504 and 9419980; registers show `_os_crash` and `-[__NSCFString UTF8String]`. HYPOTHESIS (UNVERIFIED): the signed app lacks the iCloud container entitlement, or tvOS 27.3 beta changed CloudKit. AUDIT, item 1 (every CloudKit object at or near launch): there is ONE place a `CKContainer` is made, `CloudKitGate.containers` in `App/Support/AppDependencies.swift` (lines 66 to 77), inside a `LazyCloudResource`; nothing makes it in `AppServices.init` in a Release build (the one call at AppServices.swift:180 is `#if DEBUG`, UI-test modes). It is made by the first `applyCloudGate()` (AppServices.swift:187), called from `refreshCloudStatus()` (:462, started by `Task { await refreshCloudStatus() }` at :429) and from `startCloudSync()` (:508, started at :433, after `online.reconcileKeys()`); both are started by `launch(scanOnLaunch:)`, which RootView's `.task` calls (RootView.swift:151) once the first frame is up. `AppServices` is `@MainActor`, so both tasks run on the MAIN thread: `applyCloudGate()` is the one place that can reach CloudKit on the main thread within 0.4 s of launch, and the crash's thread is the main thread (inferred, no symbols). Everything else is later or off the main thread: `container.accountStatus()` (CloudKitUserDataCloud.swift:36 to 41, CloudKitBackupCloud.swift:42 to 45, UserDataSyncEngine.sync, SyncEngine.swift:71) runs after the container exists and in `async` code off the main actor; `CloudSyncHost.start` (CloudSyncHost.swift:44) only watches a notification by name; `request(.becameActive)` (RootView.swift:154) goes to the coordinator actor; `PreferenceSync` and `SettingsSync` (R456, R457) read and write the ledger and UserDefaults only; Settings and Parental Controls use the Keychain (`KeychainPINStore`, `kSecAttrSynchronizable` false) or the container only after a tap (Sync Now). WHAT CHECKS FIRST: nothing checks the account or the entitlement before the container is made (the account status is a method of the container itself); the only guard is `VPCatchException`, an Objective-C `@try/@catch (NSException)`, which catches a RAISED exception and cannot catch a trap: a crash that arrives as EXC_BREAKPOINT from `_os_crash` passes through it. That fits the R266 comment ("what happens otherwise is not documented"): the gate was built for an exception, the device shows a trap. AUDIT, item 2 (changes between ad96c52, build 202611232102 on Codemagic, and aca3d0b): App code on the launch, CloudKit or sync path: AppServices.init gained `restrictionsOn = ParentalKeys.read().enabled` and `parental = ParentalPIN(store: KeychainPINStore())` (UserDefaults read and a struct; no Keychain call at init); `launch()` gained `await applyRestrictions()` (before `startCloudSync`) and `sync.onSynced = ...`; `startCloudSync()` and `reapplyUserData()` gained `reconcileSettings()` (R456, R457: after the sync); `CloudSyncHost` gained the `onSynced` callback; `SettingsSync.swift` is new; RootView leaves out the Files tab while Restrictions are on; `Package.swift`: AppLogic now depends on LibraryBackup (a link edge, no new call); the package gained `UserData/Edits.swift`, `Schema.swift` (+4 lines), `AppLogic/AppPreferences.swift` (PreferenceSync) and `LibraryBackup` changes (user artwork in the backup). UNCHANGED since ad96c52: `AppDependencies.swift`, `CloudKitGate`, `LazyCloudResource`, `CloudKitContainers`, the three CloudKit adapters, `VPCatchException`, `project.yml` (so the entitlements, generated into App/VideoPlayer.entitlements at each build), `TopShelf/`. Build tooling: the TestFlight route moved from Codemagic to GitHub (e8b4b99, R463): same scripts (`cm-testflight.sh`, `cm-signing.py`, `cm-profile-check.py`), a different Mac image, certificate import and CLI install (`gh-testflight.sh`), and the profiles are taken or made through the App Store Connect API the same way. READING: no changed app code touches CloudKit, so the code gives no cause; what differs is the machine and the signing route (and the OS the build was tried on, which this record does not know: the owner is asked whether ad96c52's build launched on this Apple TV and this tvOS). WHAT THIS COMMIT DOES: (1) `scripts/gh-entitlement-check.py`, run by `gh-testflight.sh check-entitlements`, a step of testflight.yml after the archive and before the export: read-only (`codesign -d --entitlements :-`, `security cms -D -i` on the embedded profile), prints only yes or no for the iCloud container, the iCloud services (CloudKit), the App Group and aps-environment, signed and in the profile, for the app and the Top Shelf extension, never a value, and fails the run before the export and the upload when the app lacks the container or the CloudKit service (signed, or in a readable profile; an unreadable profile prints n/a and does not fail, because its place in a tvOS bundle is UNVERIFIED). (2) a second box, `keep_dsyms` (default off, 1 day), keeps the archive's dSYMs folder as the artifact `testflight-dsyms`: a dSYM holds debug symbols (function, type and file names, line tables, build paths, the build's UUID), not the source text, the profile, the entitlements, the Team ID or any key; the repository's source is public already, and Apple receives the symbols with the upload anyway (`uploadSymbols` true in the export options); the cost is that anyone signed in to GitHub can download the artifact for a day, so it stays off unless a crash needs it. With the dSYM, the offsets of the crash report can be turned into function names; `atos` needs a Mac, and on 24 Nov 2026 WSL here had neither `llvm-symbolizer` nor `lldb` (only python3 was found), so installing one would be outside contact and is left to the owner. NOT CHANGED: the app's CloudKit code (the audit finds no clear cause). WHAT CANNOT BE PROVEN ON LINUX: that the signed app lacks the entitlement (that is exactly what the new step will say on the next run); that codesign's and security's output on the runner has the form the check reads; that `embedded.mobileprovision` is where the check looks in a tvOS archive; that the crash is not a tvOS 27.3 beta change; what the crash's frames are (no dSYM for 202611242205 exists; the build's symbols were not kept). | PROVISIONAL: check and dSYM box added as text; tests pass on Linux (the check on synthetic plists); never run on a Mac; the cause is unknown. | (a) change the gate so CloudKit is not touched on the main thread at launch (made on a background task, or only after the first Settings visit): changes when sync starts and still cannot catch a trap; (b) skip CloudKit when the signed build looks wrong, by reading the app's own entitlements at run time (no public tvOS API was found; not done); (c) keep dSYMs always (rejected: the artifact is public); (d) read the symbols from App Store Connect instead of the artifact (needs a Mac or the web page; UNVERIFIED that it offers them); (e) compare with ad96c52's build on the same Apple TV first (the owner's answer decides whether the code or the signing route changed) |
| R467 | **The entitlement check of R466 could not read anything on the runner, and it stopped the run (owner's report, 24 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NEVER RUN ON A MAC).** (Moved to the exported .ipa: see R468.) EVIDENCE: TestFlight workflow runs #4 and #5 (commit 0bcf4b0) failed at the step "Check the signed entitlements of the archive"; the log of run #5 shows `all keys n/a (could not be read)` for app/signed, app/profile, topshelf/signed and topshelf/profile, then `ENTITLEMENT ERROR: the signed entitlements of the app could not be read`; nothing was exported or uploaded. Even the Top Shelf extension printed n/a, so the READING failed, not the app's contents. CAUSE: unknown; only hypotheses (UNVERIFIED): `codesign -d --entitlements :-` gave no plist on stdout for the archived bundle (a different output form, the plist on stderr, or the bundle not yet signed in the archive), and `embedded.mobileprovision` is not at the bundle's top level (or `security cms` failed) in a tvOS archive. The first version of the check printed nothing that says which; that is fixed below. CHANGES (`scripts/gh-entitlement-check.py`, `scripts/gh-testflight.sh`, the workflow step name and comments, `docs/TESTFLIGHT.md`, `scripts/test-ci-testflight-log.sh`): (1) the signed entitlements are read in three forms, in this order, and the first that gives a plist wins: `codesign -d --entitlements :- BUNDLE`, then `codesign -d --entitlements - --xml BUNDLE` (stdout and stderr are both looked at, neither is ever printed), then `BUNDLE/archived-expanded-entitlements.xcent` (the file Xcode writes into an archived app, read with plistlib); each read prints one fixed word as its source: `codesign` or `xcent` (the profile's is `profile`). (2) When no form reads a bundle's signed entitlements, the log gets only safe facts: the exit codes of the two codesign calls (numbers, or `not-run`), whether `embedded.mobileprovision` exists (yes or no), whether `archived-expanded-entitlements.xcent` exists (yes or no), and the bundle's top-level file NAMES, but only names on a fixed list that the repository's scripts already use for these bundles (`Info.plist`, `PlugIns`, `_CodeSignature`, `Frameworks`, `PkgInfo`, `Assets.car`, `embedded.mobileprovision`, the .xcent file, the bundle's own executable name) or that end in `.lproj`, `.bundle`, `.storyboardc`, `.nib`, `.car`, `.appex`, `.framework`, and only if the name does not look like it carries an identifier (ten capital letters or digits in a row, 32 hex digits, a UUID start, an at sign, a space or a colon, or more than 48 characters); every other name is only COUNTED (`+N other names, not shown`), so a Team ID, a profile name or a certificate name cannot reach the log through a file name. Never a value, never a file's content. (3) UNREADABLE IS NOT A FAILURE: when the app's signed entitlements cannot be read by any form, the keys print `n/a`, ONE `WARNING:` line says so, the last line says `not decided (n/a); this is NOT a pass`, and the exit code is 0, so the export and the upload go on. The exit code is 1 ONLY when the app's signed entitlements WERE read and lack the iCloud container or the CloudKit service. A readable embedded profile that lacks either is now a `WARNING:` line, not a failure (R466 failed on it; this is the owner's rule for R467). (4) Tests: the .xcent fallback (codesign output unparseable, file present: reads yes, exit 0), nothing readable (n/a, one warning, safe facts, names counted, exit 0), readable and missing the container (exit 1), readable and complete (exit 0), the second codesign form, an unreadable Top Shelf alone; the sentinel values (Team ID, profile and certificate names, file names) never reach the log. Stand-in `codesign` and `security` scripts are used on Linux. RISK of (3): a signed app WITHOUT iCloud now passes this step whenever the reading fails; the WARNING and the facts are the only trace, and the crash of build 202611242205 stays unexplained until the next run shows the facts or the entitlements. CANNOT BE PROVEN ON LINUX: what codesign prints on the runner (the stand-ins are my guess of its forms), that the .xcent file is in the archived tvOS app, what the top-level names are, that the bundle is signed at archive time. | PROVISIONAL: text and stand-in tests only; never run on a Mac. | (a) keep failing when nothing can be read (rejected by the owner: a diagnostic must not block an upload that may be fine); (b) check the exported .ipa after the export instead (unzip it and read the same way; the export re-signs, so it is the closer evidence, but it comes after the export and before the upload only); (c) read the code signature with another tool (`codesign --display --xml`, `plutil`, `otool`; none verified); (d) print the facts only and never decide; (e) drop the step and read the entitlements on a Mac (the owner has none) |

| R469 | **Why the signed app had no entitlements, and the fix (owner's request, 24 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NEVER RUN ON A MAC).** EVIDENCE: run #6 (099ee39): the ARCHIVED app and Top Shelf had no _CodeSignature, no embedded.mobileprovision, no .xcent. Run #7 (6447ae9): in the exported .ipa the embedded profiles of both targets carry all four keys (yes), but the SIGNED entitlements of BOTH targets are all no, even the committed Top Shelf application-groups key. Builds 202611242205 and 202611250008 crash in CloudKit at launch. The Codemagic route (same scripts) did sign entitlements: App Store Connect error 90575 on Codemagic build 3 complained about aps-environment because the upload carried icloud-services (R266). TRACE: (1) project.yml: both targets have `entitlements:`; XcodeGen's ProjectSpec says it writes the file "and use it by setting CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS build setting for every configuration" (Release included; the generator source sets it per configuration). Not the cause. (2) Archive command (cm-testflight.sh archive, both routes): Release, generic tvOS, CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION only; no CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED, CODE_SIGNING_REQUIRED, CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY, CODE_SIGN_STYLE, PROVISIONING_PROFILE_SPECIFIER, OTHER_CODE_SIGN_FLAGS or CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS override; the workflow step adds nothing (CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO exists only in compile-check.yml and ci-build-uitests.sh). (3) `xcode-project use-profiles` (codemagic-cli-tools source, read on GitHub): it lists certificates with `security find-certificate` in the DEFAULT keychain only (Keychain() with no path runs `security default-keychain`; no keychain option); for a manual profile it writes CODE_SIGN_STYLE Manual, DEVELOPMENT_TEAM, PROVISIONING_PROFILE_SPECIFIER and CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY = the common name of the profile certificate FOUND in that keychain, and when none is found it still writes the profile with CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY = "" and exits 0; it never touches CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS; the export options take signingCertificate from the profile, not the keychain. (4) gh-testflight.sh install-cert put the certificate in a temporary keychain added to the SEARCH LIST but never made the DEFAULT; on Codemagic the platform's own keychain is the default. (5) cm-testflight.sh checked only CODE_SIGN_STYLE and the profile after use-profiles, not the identity. CAUSE (from the above; the last link unverified): GitHub route only: use-profiles found no certificate in the default keychain, wrote an empty CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY, Xcode archived both targets without signing them (an empty identity meaning "do not sign" is NOT stated on Apple's build settings page, which says only that a missing or invalid certificate is a build error; unverified), and the export then signed the unsigned binaries with the right profile and certificate (found on the search list) but with no entitlements, because none were in the archive (Apple's Distributing page does not say which entitlements an export uses; unverified). This explains every fact of runs #6 and #7, both targets alike. FIX (scripts only, smallest): install-cert also runs `security default-keychain -d user -s` on the temporary keychain, checks it, and saves the old default, which cleanup restores before deleting the keychain; the archive step (both routes) now stops before `xcodebuild archive` when use-profiles left a target with an empty identity (prints set/empty only). No signing setting is widened: no override of CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY, CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS or the style. NEW STEP: `gh-testflight.sh check-archive` after the archive, before the export: per target signed yes/no (_CodeSignature) and Release CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS yes/no with the base name only when it is VideoPlayer.entitlements or TopShelf.entitlements (else "other"); exit 1 if any is no. R468 check unchanged. Tests: stand-in xcode-project and xcodebuild (empty identity stops before the archive; check-archive yes/no lines, exit codes, no path or name printed) and static checks of install-cert and cleanup (102 passed). NOT PROVEN on Linux: that the runner accepts the default-keychain change; that use-profiles then finds the certificate; that Xcode signs the archive with the entitlements; the real -showBuildSettings output; anything on a Mac. | PROVISIONAL. Rejected alternatives: (a) pass CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY="Apple Distribution" on the archive command (works around use-profiles instead of fixing the keychain, and overrides every target, the package targets included: wider signing); (b) pass CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS on the command line (already set by XcodeGen; would not make an unsigned archive signed); (c) re-sign the exported app with codesign and the entitlements files (custom signing, outside Xcode's export, error-prone); (d) a `keychain` command from the Codemagic CLI (initialize/add-certificates) to mirror Codemagic exactly (the pre-push check refuses add-certificates in the GitHub route, R463; more change); (e) automatic or cloud-managed signing (R464; needs a registered device, rejected before); (f) only warn in check-archive instead of stopping (an unsigned archive can never give a working app, so the export would be wasted) |
| R470 | **Text over artwork still unreadable on a real picture (owner's photo of build 202611250008, episode "S4 E15", 24 Nov 2026; owner's rule of 23 Nov, R312; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** EVIDENCE: the series line and the title over the bright fountain; the cover starting abruptly under the title row; "TV-MA 1080p" borderline; the description and the buttons fine. CAUSES: (1) the anchor was `TitleHeader` (`reportsArtTextTop` on the title), not the text column, and the episode's series line sits above the title; (2) `ArtTextTop` kept the LARGEST global top seen (to ignore scrolling), but the hero column is bottom-aligned and grows UPWARD when the details, the genres or the title logo arrive, so the cover stayed at the column's old, lower top; (3) the ramp was 0.12 of the height with two linear stops, a visible step; (4) `coverStart` clamped the start at 0, so a column near the top of the screen (the show page's hero is only `InfoBlockLayout.showHeroFraction` 0.30 of the page) had its title in the fading part. GEOMETRY BEFORE: start = title top / height - 0.12 (clamped at 0), black 0 -> 0.80 linear over 0.12, the material mask clear -> opaque over the same range, anchor = the title's top, largest value seen. GEOMETRY AFTER (a501787): start = column top / height - 0.25 (clamped only at -0.25 and 1), black 0 -> 0.80 on a smootherstep curve sampled into 9 stops over 0.25 (about 270 pt), only the part on the picture drawn, the material mask on the same curve (opacity / 0.80), full strength from the column's first line down; anchor = the top of the whole text column (episode and movie hero `VStack` with the series line, show and season hero, Home's featured column), measured in the scroll content's own space plus the scroll view's top and its top content inset, latest value (scrolling does not move it). Page layouts unchanged. MODEL (d9dcf7b, `ArtReadabilityTests`, 1080 pt screen, HIG tvOS type sizes): column tops episode 282 pt (cover from 12 pt), movie 346 (from 76), show and season 60 (full strength from the top edge: no clear picture above the text on that page, by its layout), Home 533 (from 263); every block of every page passes over pure white and over a photograph (white top, mid grey middle), worst 5.08:1 (secondary text); the old cover fails the series line at 1.82:1, and with the stale anchor the title at 1.0:1. Library, Search: captions under the art, not over it (only the progress scrim, unchanged); the player's panels are the system's; Top Shelf excluded. UNVERIFIED: the real column positions and the scroll view's content inset on tvOS 27 (the reader's sum is from Apple's `ScrollGeometry` page, not measured), how the material renders, and whether the eased ramp shows no edge by eye; the owner's next photo decides. | (a) A thicker system material (`thickMaterial`) instead of more black (Apple states no darkening, so the proof could not count it). (b) A text shadow or vibrancy on the text instead of a stronger cover. (c) A full-height dim of the whole picture (simplest; loses the bright picture above the text). (d) A fixed share again (0.35) with the long eased ramp (no measuring; wrong whenever a column is taller). (e) A shorter ramp (0.15 to 0.2) so more of the picture above the text stays clear on the episode page (its cover starts at 12 pt in the model). (f) Per-image brightness sampling (R452 (b)). |
| R472 | **Plex MKV (HEVC Main 10 hvc1.2.4.L120.90, 1920 x 1080, E-AC-3) on the Converted path never became ready (owner's photos of Last Playback Problem, build 202611250008 on 96ebfea, 24 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** EVIDENCE: file opened 0.3 s, loopback ready 0.3 s, item created 0.8 s, nothing after; Error Code CoreMediaErrorDomain -16847, no underlying error; Last Event "HTTP 500 · video at 0:00 · 25 s after the player opened"; the step's error is OUR 30 s limit for "Waiting for the system player to open the file", not a failure the player reported. A loopback 500 means the session's handler threw (RemuxHost.start, Loopback.runHandler). TRACE (read only): prepare() reads the index and the FIRST window (all offered tracks) and keeps it, so the playlists, both init segments and segment 0 (video and audio) read nothing more; segment 1 reads a 32-byte cluster head and its window (Demuxer.window); every read goes through PlexClient.read (Range request; 200 -> rangesNotSupported, 416 -> rangeNotSatisfiable, other -> httpStatus(n); the whole range body is held in memory by URLSession `data(for:)`; `timeoutIntervalForRequest` 30 s is an idle timeout). REPRODUCTION (RemuxEngineTests/ConvertedStartR472Tests, synthetic file of the owner's shape, the first resources in the player's order, logged as RemuxHost logs them): (a) normal reader: every first resource 200 at once, codec string hvc1.2.4.L120.90, E-AC-3 passed through as ec-3; (b) slow reads: all 200, time = reads x latency (segment 1 needs 2 to 3 reads, so 25 s would need about 10 s per read, a stalled server); (c) HTTP 500 from the server once the file is open: segment 1 answers 500 AT ONCE ("httpStatus(500)"), everything before it 200; (d) 200 instead of 206: from the first read the file never opens (the photo shows it opened); after the open, segment 1 answers 500 at once ("rangesNotSupported"); (e) a stalled read: segment 1 answers 500 after the transport's timeout (about 30 s on the device, NSURLErrorDomain -1001). NONE matches the photo (the player never became ready, while every case serves segment 0; only (e) gives a late 500, at about 30 s, not 25). So NO behaviour change. CHANGE (step 3, second branch): the Last Event row now ends with "Loopback <resource>: <thrown text>" (the first resource answered 500, from LoopbackServeLog.firstFailure, redacted by LoopbackServeLog.redact and again by PlaybackRedaction with the source's secrets), so one photo of the first screen names what failed and why. UNVERIFIED without a device: how AVPlayer retries a segment that answered 500 (Apple's pages read, AVPlayerItem.status and HTTP Live Streaming, state none) and how much it needs before readyToPlay; whether the real file's SPS, Cues or E-AC-3 frames differ from the synthetic ones. | (a) Also show the whole serve log (every first resource and its answer) on the first screen instead of only the first failure (longer first screen). (b) Answer a failed segment with a status AVPlayer does not retry (for example 404) so the start fails at once instead of at our 30 s limit (changes behaviour before the cause is known). (c) Serve the stream through AVAssetResourceLoader instead of the loopback, so the exact failing request reaches the app (a large change). (d) A test build that logs every loopback request to the console for a cable-connected Mac (the owner has none). |
| R471 (1) | **Product pages below the hero: centred title and the blurred, dimmed artwork (owner's request R471, Part 2 (g); frames of the Apple TV app on tvOS 27: episode t02s and t04s, show t06s, t12s and t14s, movie t02s; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** AUDIT (Part 1, accepted by the owner): the Apple TV app keeps the artwork behind the whole page; once the page is scrolled below its first screen, the artwork is a blurred, dimmed copy, and the part below starts with the title or clear logo centred at the top, scrolling with the content (t04s shows it moving up). Ours kept the clear artwork and had no title below the hero. The scroll position is read with `onScrollGeometryChange`. UNVERIFIED without a device: the look of the material on tvOS 27; whether focus moving one row at a time scrolls without stutter (no scroll-target snapping is set, the system's focus scrolling decides); the exact share at which the frames switch. | (a) Snap the page to rows (`scrollTargetBehavior(.viewAligned)` with a scroll-target layout), as the frames suggest one row per press (may fight the focus engine's own scrolling; needs a device). (b) A fixed header that stays at the top while scrolling (the frames show it scrolling away, t16s). (c) A real blur of the picture (`blur`) instead of the material (custom, not a system material; HIG Materials prefers the system ones). It always asks first (system confirmation dialog, destructive button) with our text saying what changes and what stays (`Unmatcher.confirmation`: the online description, pictures and credits go and it is not matched again automatically; the file(s), progress and edits stay; Fix Match can match it again). What it does (AppLogic `Unmatcher`): removes the online id (`LibraryStore.clearMatch`, only `tmdb_id`; title, files, watch state unchanged); moves edits kept under the online id to the keys the title has without one (movie and episode: the file key; show and seasons: the show's name keys, R449), with their stamps; removes the saved Fix Match choices covering the files (`StoredMatchForgetter`, matches.json and its iCloud records, R381); holds each file unmatched (user record type "unmatch", synced and backed up like other user records), and the automatic matcher (`TMDBArtworkProvider`) then takes only a saved Fix Match choice for a held file, so a later Fix Match still works. NOT CHANGED: a title or year that Fix Match wrote on a movie stays (the guessed one is not restored); an id read from the user's own NFO file comes back on the next scan (the source's file wins). | R471 (15) | **Delete from Library for a movie, an episode, a season and a whole show; the library only (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 2; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: `ProductAction.delete` ("Delete from Library") is built: in Manage on the movie and episode page, in a movie or episode card's long press, and in the show page's Manage (the whole show, and the season on screen when the show has more than one). It always asks first (system confirmation dialog, destructive button; `LibraryRemover.confirmation`): the title is removed from this app's library, the file stays on the source and is not deleted there, later scans do not add it back, and where to bring it back. Off while Restrictions are on (`ProductActionContext.deleteAllowed`, and `AppServices.removeFromLibrary` refuses), as Fix Match (R460). What it does (AppLogic `LibraryRemover`): records each file in the user layer (record type "libraryremoval", synced and backed up like other user records), and marks the files removed in the index (`LibraryStore.removeFromLibrary`; the item rows and their watch state stay, so a returning file finds them). The scan skips those paths (`SortOverrideProviding.excludedPaths`, read from the ledger by `LedgerSortOverrides`). Settings > Library > Removed from Library (new, `RemovedFromLibraryView`, off while Restrictions are on, as Needs Review) lists them, and Bring Back to Library removes the record and scans again. NOT BUILT (open): deleting the file on the source. Tests: LibraryRemovalTests (5), ProductActionsTests, FixMatchScopeTests, ShowSeasonEditTests, UnmatchTests follow. | R471 (16) | **Rate: the user's own stars for a movie, episode, show and season (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 3; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: `ProductAction.rate` is built and now listed for every kind (it was on the movie's list only; R471 (8) had it off), after Add to Playlist or Collection. The entry is a system menu picker (`RateMenu`: 5 Stars down to 1 Star, then No Rating; the current one checked and named in the entry, "Rate: 4 Stars"), on the movie and episode page's details button, in a movie or episode card's long press (the card does not read the current rating, so nothing is checked there), and on the show page for the show and for the season on screen ("Rate Season 2"). Stored in the user layer (UserData `UDRating`, record type "rating", field "stars", null when cleared), keyed like edits (`EditTarget.keys`, `showKeys`, `seasonKeys`), newest stamp across the keys wins; synced and backed up under the R456 rules like every user record. WRITTEN BACK to the source only where the source keeps ratings and the user turned its two-way sync switch on (the R309 switch): Plex only, for a movie's or an episode's own files (`ServerRatingSender`, `PlexWatchWriter.rate`: `PUT /:/rate` with identifier, the ratingKey and 0 to 10, one star = 2, -1 clears). UNVERIFIED: that path and its parameters were not read on Plex's reference this session (only Apple's pages may be read); a refused request is counted and the rating stays in the app. NOT SENT: show and season ratings (no server key for them here); Jellyfin and Emby (they keep user ratings too; not built); WebDAV, SMB and the other file sources (nowhere to write). NOT BUILT (open): written reviews. Tests: RatingTests (5), PlexWatchWriterTests (rating request), ProductActionsTests follow. | R471 (17) | **Add to List and Add to Playlist or Collection: the user's own lists of library titles (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 4; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: both actions are built. Lists live in the user layer as two plain record types, "userlist" (name, kind list / playlist / collection, created) and "userlistentry" (one per title in a list: the title's stable library key, `items.key` for a movie or an episode, `series.key` for a show, and its name when added), synced and backed up like every other record (R456: an unregistered type merges field by field, the later stamp wins; deleting writes a deletion stamp). Add to List offers the lists of kind list; Add to Playlist or Collection offers playlists and collections, each named with its kind. Both menus end with New List… / New Playlist… / New Collection…, which open the shared one-field name page (`TextEntryPage`) and put the title in the new list. On a movie, episode or show page the lists holding the title are checked and choosing one adds or removes it; on a card's long press nothing is checked and choosing a list adds (the card reads the title's key only when a list is chosen). Settings > Library > My Lists (hidden with the other title lists while Restrictions are on) shows the lists per kind with their counts, opens one on its titles (Open, Remove from List; a title not in this device's library says so), and renames or deletes a list after a confirmation that says the titles stay in the library. A season is not a list entry of its own: the season page no longer lists the two actions. | R471 (18) | **Refresh Details for one title (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 5; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: `ProductAction.refreshMetadata` ("Refresh Details") is built, in Manage of a movie and of a show (the episode's and season's Manage lists do not have it, as before). It is a real refetch, not a page reload: `TitleRefresher` drops the online answers kept for this title only (TMDB's `tmdb/movie/<id>/` or `tmdb/tv/<id>/`, which holds a show's seasons and episodes too; the free data source's entries by that id and by the title's name, every year), then `OnlineServices.refreshTitle` builds the lookups again (no remembered "found nothing") and fetches the page's details and picture now through the same layers in the same order (`LayeredDetailsService`, the artwork engine). An alert says what came back ("Details Refreshed" or "Nothing New Found"). The user's own edits, chosen pictures and Fix Match live in the user layer and are applied on top when the page reloads (R449 to R451); other titles are not touched. | R471 (19) | **Analyze File for one movie or episode (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 6; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: `ProductAction.analyze` is built, in Manage wherever the title has a file. It opens a cover (`AnalyzeFileView`, `SettingsPane`) that starts at once on the file the player opens (the preferred version): step 1 reads the file's streams again with the Media Info header reader (`MediaTrackProbe`, Matroska and WebM; the result replaces the kept one), step 2, for an episode, finds its intro and credits again with the audio comparison of R316 (`EpisodeAudioReader`), run even when this file was analysed before (`SegmentProvider.analysisTarget(force:)`; the new record replaces the old one in its season). The step under way is named in the status lines with the system progress indicator; at the end the status lines say what was found (audio and subtitle tracks, the intro and/or credits, or that none were found yet because the comparison needs another episode of the season). Analyze Again runs it once more. Nothing else is read or changed. Tests: `FileAnalysisTests` (5). | Not read here: MP4, MOV and TS headers (the player reads them when it plays; the package cannot use the system media framework on Linux, R192). A movie has no intro or credits step (detection is per season). Alternatives: analyze the whole season from a season's Manage; analyze every version, not only the preferred one; run in the background with a notice instead of a cover; also re-read the chapters and sidecar segment files (they are read live at playback today). | R471 (20) | **Play History for one movie or episode (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 7; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** CHANGE: `ProductAction.playHistory` is built, in Manage of a movie and an episode (a show or a season has no history of its own: its episodes do). The app did not keep plays until now (only the watch state: position, watched, play count, last played), so each playback is now recorded in the user layer: record type "playhistory", one record per playback, id "<item key>\|<device>\|<start>", fields itemKey, device, startedAt, lastAt, position, duration, finished; written from the player's progress saves only (`AppServices.saveProgress`; marking watched by hand is not a playback). A save less than 30 minutes after the last one of the same item on the same device continues that playback; a later one starts a new entry; 50 entries are kept per item. Finished: at or past the library's watched point (95 %). Synced and backed up like every other record (R456). The page opens a read-only cover (`PlayHistoryView`): each playback's start (system date and time style) and "Finished" or "Stopped at 30:30 of 1:40:00", newest first; Clear Play History asks first, says that the watched state, progress and play count stay, and removes the entries here and on the user's other devices. Tests: `PlayHistoryTests` (6). | Plays before this version are not listed (there was no record of them). Alternatives: a gap of 5, 15 or 60 minutes; keep 20 or all entries; name the device of each entry; a show's history as its episodes' entries together; Clear also resetting the watch state (Plex-like "clear history"); a history of the whole library under Settings. UNVERIFIED on a device: how often the player saves (every save is one write to the ledger). |

| R471 (24) | **Play trailers and bonus content from their shelves (owner's request R471, Part 3 item 11; wording ours; 25 Nov 2026; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** FOUND: the Trailers and Bonus Content shelves (R471 (7)) could never fill: the library scan treated "Title-trailer.mkv" as a second version of the title (or as a title of its own), and a title's sidecars listed no videos. CHANGE: (1) the scan knows an extra video (`ExtraVideo`): a name ending in a tag after a hyphen ("-trailer", "-teaser" are trailers; "-featurette", "-behindthescenes", "-deleted", "-deletedscene", "-interview", "-scene", "-short", "-clip", "-extra" are bonus content), or a video in a folder named "Trailers" (trailers), "Featurettes", "Extras", "Behind The Scenes", "Deleted Scenes" or "Interviews" (bonus content), any case. Such a file gets no item and is not sorted; one stored as a title before is taken off that item on the next scan. A file with a sort override of its own (sorted by hand) stays a title. (2) A title's sidecars include the extra videos named after it in its folder, and the videos in an extras folder next to its file; when that folder holds more than one title, only the extras whose name starts with the title's file name. (3) Selecting a card plays the file in the system player, from its start (`ExtraPlayerScreen`): MKV and the other containers the converting player opens go through it, the rest plays from the source's own link, as a title's file does. Nothing is saved: no position, no watched state, no Up Next, no server watch sync. The file is looked up again among the title's own extras before it plays (`ExtraFiles.find`). (4) The card's facts (name, kind, size) move to its long-press menu (File Details, with Play); the details button's Extras menu is unchanged. Tests: `ExtraVideoTests` (3), `ExtraPlaybackTests` (2), two source checks in `ScreenChecksTests`. UNVERIFIED on a device: playback of an extra, Menu closing it, and a Plex file that the server would have to convert (the check `resolvePlayback` makes first is not made here; the system player then reports its own error). Show-level extras (a show's "Extras" folder) and a season folder's extras not named after an episode are on no page. | Alternatives: also the folders "Other", "Shorts", "Scenes" and the tag "-other" (common names of folders of real titles; left out); tags after a dot or an underscore too ("Title.trailer.mkv"); extras play in the full player with its info tabs and save their position; a show page's own Extras shelf for a show's extras folder; Select opens the facts as before and Play is in the long-press menu only; keep extras as versions (the old behaviour). |

| R471 (49) | **Library grids: every row is drawn, and one Down reaches the next row (owner's report of 25 Nov 2026: "only one row displays at a time and when i scroll down once the rows scroll down at least 3 and skip several items that i cant see"; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT TRIED on a device).** SCREEN BELIEVED MEANT: the Movies and Shows library grids (and a person's titles, the same `PosterGridLayout`). They are the only screens that hide rows: R443 (commit 6995168, owner's device test of 23 Nov 2026, item 2) drew a card only when at least 98 % of it was on screen (`WholeRowOnly`, opacity 0 from `onScrollVisibilityChange(threshold: 0.98)`). With rows 660 pt apart on a 1080 pt screen only one row is ever 98 % visible, so one row showed at a time. CAUSE OF THE SKIP (our reading, UNVERIFIED on tvOS 27): a hidden card is not a focus candidate, while a row built below the screen that has never been on it keeps its starting state (drawn), so one Down passed over the hidden rows to the first row never yet seen, about three rows on. The other causes tested in the code: no lazy-row gap elsewhere (product pages are a plain VStack, Home a LazyVStack of whole shelves, no shelf hidden); no paging or view-aligned scroll behaviour and no `scrollPosition` anywhere; `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` only on Jump to Letter, not on focus; the grid's single `focusSection()` (R361) and the product pages' hero sections (R424) are kept; the R471 header publishes only on half-point changes and its backdrop is the page's background, so it does not move rows. CHANGED: `WholeRowOnly` and its environment key are removed (PosterGridLayout, PosterCard, SeriesCard); R443 item 1 (the controls are the grid's header and scroll away) stays, so no row sits under them; R443's half-row rule now applies only while the controls are on screen. New rules `GridScrollRules.downReachesNextRow`, `focusedRowWhole`, `moreThanOneRowShown` (Linux tests with the old behaviour failing: `testDownSkippingRowsFails`, `testMoreThanOneRowIsShown`, `testTheFocusedRowIsWholeOnScreen`; source check `testNoGridCardIsHiddenWhileScrolling`); test16b applies them after each Down on Movies and Shows. NOT TESTED HERE: the focus engine itself (it needs tvOS). FRAMES REREAD (Part B item 3): the Home hero photo shows the first shelf cut by the screen's lower edge, and show frame t12s the row under the focused episodes (Trailers) cut the same way: the reference app draws a partly visible next row, as the grids now do. HOW TO TELL: the owner names the screen or sends a new recording; on the next build, Down on Movies should show the focused row whole with the top of the next row under it. A build could log the focused card's row index and the page's scroll offset on each focus change into a diagnostics page (as Playback's last event), so a photo shows which rows were passed over. | (a) as built: draw every row, let the focus engine scroll the focused row into view; (b) keep hiding partial rows but only at the bottom edge, never above the focused row; (c) scroll by rows with `scrollTargetLayout()` and `scrollTargetBehavior(.viewAligned)` (R443 alternative (d)); (d) smaller posters (six columns) so two whole rows fit; (e) a non-lazy grid for libraries under about 30 rows; (f) another screen was meant (Home's shelves, a product page's shelves, Search, Settings): the owner says which; (g) scroll exactly one row per Down press (`scrollTargetLayout()` with `.viewAligned`, or a step of one row pitch). NOT TAKEN (25 Nov 2026, second Part B item 2): in the reference frames a Down press moves the focus to the next row and the page scrolls only as far as it needs to show that row whole, the row after it partly in view (Home hero photo: the first shelf cut by the lower edge; show t12s: Trailers under the focused episodes), which is what the focus engine does without a snap; a row-aligned snap stays here for the owner. |
| R471 (50) | **Scroll cost per frame: audit and the fixes that keep the look (owner 25 Nov 2026 Part B item 2; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT MEASURED on a device).** AUDIT (read from the code): (1) the page backdrop is the scroll view's `.background`, so it does not move or re-measure while the page scrolls; (2) `ArtTextTop` measures the text column in the scroll CONTENT's space (constant while scrolling) and publishes on half-point changes; (3) `reportsBelowHero` computes a Bool from the scroll offset each frame (cheap) and writes only when it flips; (4) page height, caption heights and `FittingText` already wrote only on half-point changes; (5) image decoding at display size: R471 (48); (6) containers: Home `ScrollView > LazyVStack` of `LazyHStack` shelves; movie, episode and show pages a plain `VStack` of fewer than ten sections, each shelf a `LazyHStack`; library grids `LazyVGrid`; no shelf is a non-lazy row of many cards. FOUND AND CHANGED: (a) the below-hero material and its cover stayed in the tree at opacity 0 over the whole hero, a second full-screen material on every frame: now in the tree only while it shows, with the same fade (`.transition(.opacity)`); (b) `BackdropView`'s own frame and `FittingLine`'s widths were written on any change: now only on half a point or more. Test: `testScrollingWritesAndLayersAreKeptDown`. NOT CHANGED (would change the look or needs a device): the hero's masked live material (the blur itself; a pre-blurred copy decoded once would look different, alternative (b)); below the hero the masked material still sits under the full one (removing it changes the lower part's blur slightly, alternative (c)); the episode page keeps `.blur(radius: 0)` when spoilers are off (believed a no-op, UNVERIFIED). HOW TO MEASURE without Instruments: a build could count frames that take longer than 1/60 s while a page scrolls (a `CADisplayLink` timestamp gap) and show the count, the worst gap and the screen's name on the diagnostics page next to Playback's last event, so a photo of that page after scrolling tells which screen is slow. | (a) as built; (b) cache the blur once: a blurred, downsampled copy of the picture made with Core Image when it loads, in place of the live material below the hero; (c) below the hero, drop the masked material under the full one; (d) a `LazyVStack` for the product pages (risk: rows not yet built take no focus, see R471 (49)); (e) leave all as it was until a device measures it. |
| R471 (51) | **Focus moves and scrolling speed on Settings > Playback (owner 25 Nov 2026, second Part B item 1; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT TRIED on a device).** Two sections under Last Playback Problem. FOCUS MOVES: on Movies, Shows, Search, a person's titles and Home's shelves, each focus move is kept with our own screen name, the row and column (from 1; on Home the row is the shelf, the featured area not counted) and the page's vertical scroll offset; the last 20, newest first, and a move that passes over rows says how many ("2 rows passed over"). When an older walk failed after a newer one had started (`refresh()`, or a listing that expired while a slow walk ran), the newer walk was dropped and the next lookup (a playback start, a browse) walked the whole library a third time. An older walk that finished late with success also overwrote the newer listing kept for answers without a request (`heldTree`, R380). CHANGED: both are done only when the task is still the client's current walk. | R471 (65) | **Player-side review, finding P1: one Plex client per saved source, so a playback start no longer walks the whole library twice (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 7, Part B item 2; work that repeated; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT MEASURED on a device).** CORRECTED by R471 (69): the FOUND below was wrong, because `PlexClient.shared` (R305) already gave every build of a source the same client; this row changed no behaviour, and the scan's forget had no effect until R471 (69). FOUND: `SourceFactory.source(for:)` built a new `PlexClient` on every call, and a start makes two: `plexConversionNeeded` (step "Checking the server") and `byteReader` (step "Opening the converter", `MediaSourceReader.open` asks `info`). Each new client read the server's listing from nothing: `/library/sections`, every page of every movie and show library, and one `allLeaves` request per show. So every play, every Try Again and every next episode read the whole library twice inside steps with a time limit, and R305's listing cache ("browsing, scanning and starting playback within this time cost no new library requests") never served anything, because no client lived past one call. CHANGED: `LiveSourceCache` (AppLogic) keeps the live object of a Plex source while its definition (address, path, options, trusted fingerprint) and secret stay the same; a change of either builds a new one; a build that throws keeps nothing. Every other kind is still built on each call (their connections are not kept). A scan drops the kept client first (`SourceFactory.forgetLiveSource`), so it reads a fresh listing as before; the new client is then kept for the starts after it. Within 300 s of the last listing a start now costs no library request; after that, one walk (and since R471 (59) none at all for a file already being read). TESTS: `LiveSourceCacheR471Tests` (built once and kept; a new token or address builds a new one; other kinds each time; a failed build keeps nothing) and `ScreenChecksTests.testTheSourceFactoryKeepsItsPlexClients` (the factory and the scan use it); both failed before the change (the type did not exist; the factory built every client). NOT CHANGED: a removed source's client stays in memory until the app quits (it is never asked for again). | (a) as built: keep Plex clients, scans read afresh; (b) keep every kind (SMB, SFTP and the others would keep their connections open: more to verify); (c) keep the listing in a shared store instead of the client; (d) have the server check use the listing the scan stored in the library (no request at all at start) |

| R471 (68) | **A kept Plex client is let go when its source is removed or edited (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 2; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** Before: R471 (65) kept one live client per saved source in `LiveSourceCache`; an edited definition already got a new client on its next use (the kept one is compared with the definition and secret), but the old client stayed in the cache until replaced, and the client of a REMOVED source stayed until the app quit (with its library listing). NOW: `AppServices.removeSource(id:)` calls `sourceFactory.forgetLiveSource(id:)`; `AppServices.definitionSaver` is a `ForgettingDefinitionSaver` (AppLogic) around the ledger saver, which drops the source's kept client after every successful save (an edit in the source form, Plex setup, a newly trusted certificate, a rename, an option); a failed save drops nothing. The cost: after a rename or an option change the next use walks the Plex library again (it already did, since the changed definition no longer matched the kept one). STILL OPEN AFTER THIS ROW (found in item 3 of the same round, R471 (69)): the app's builder made Plex clients through `PlexClient.shared` (R305), a process-wide store keyed by address, device and token, so a forgotten client was still held there and came back on the next build; this row releases the app's own hold only. TESTS: `ForgetLiveSourceR471Tests` (a forgotten client is released, checked with a weak reference; a saved edit drops the kept client and passes the save on; a refused save keeps it) and `ScreenChecksTests.testRemovingOrEditingASourceForgetsItsKeptClient` (the app wiring, from the source). | (a) as recorded: forget on removal and after every save; (b) forget only when a connection field changes (address, token, certificate), keeping the client across a rename or an option change; (c) forget on removal only (an edit is already handled by the definition comparison); (d) no cache at all (R471 (65)'s alternative: a new client for every use) |
| R471 (69) | **Plex path review, finding C: the app builds its own Plex client, so a scan reads the server afresh and a removed source's client is let go; and a correction of R471 (65) (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 3; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** FOUND: `SourceBuilder` made Plex clients through `PlexClient.shared` (R305), a process-wide store keyed by address, device and token. So `SourceFactory.forgetLiveSource` (R471 (65), (68)) had no effect: the next build got the same client back, with its listing up to 300 s old, and the scan's "reads the server's listing afresh" was false (a file added on the server within that time was missed until the listing expired), and a removed source's client stayed in that store until the app quit. CORRECTION of R471 (65): its FOUND ("each start walked the whole library twice; R305's listing cache never served anything") was wrong, since `PlexClient.shared` already gave every build of a source the same client from R305 on (21 Nov 2026); R471 (65) changed no behaviour then, and its pre-fix test checks only that the type and the source wiring did not exist (the item 1 report of this round lists it). CHANGED: `SourceBuilder` builds `PlexClient(baseURL:token:identity:)`; `SourceFactory`'s `LiveSourceCache` is now the one place a client is kept (one per saved source, built again after a scan's forget, an edit or a removal). Setup checks (`LiveSourceConnector`) build a client of their own each time, as every other kind does. `PlexClient.shared` and its test stay in the package, unused by the app. COST: a scan now reads the whole Plex library once per scan even within 300 s of the last listing (R305 said scanning within that time costs no library request). TEST: `ScreenChecksTests.testTheBuilderMakesItsOwnPlexClientSoForgettingItWorks`; failed before the change (2 failures), passes after. | (a) as built: one client per saved source in the factory, scans read afresh; (b) back to `PlexClient.shared` (R305), and have the scan call `PlexClient.refresh()` and the removal drop the shared entry; (c) keep the listing up to 300 s for scans too (R305's wording), forgetting only on removal and edit |
| R471 (70) | **Plex path review, finding A: a library walk longer than the listing's lifetime no longer makes every lookup walk again (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 3; work that repeated; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT MEASURED on a device).** FOUND: `PlexClient.currentTree` counted the listing's 300 s (R305) from when a walk STARTED, and a lookup joined a running walk only within those 300 s. A walk that takes longer (a large library on a slow link) was stale when it arrived, so the next lookup started another full walk; `LibrarySyncer` asks `list` once per folder, so a scan of such a library started a walk per folder and might never finish; and a lookup made while a walk had run past 300 s started a second walk beside it. CHANGED: the lifetime counts from when the newest walk ARRIVED (`treeArrived`, set only by the walk that is still current, R471 (64)); a lookup while a walk runs always joins it. TRADE-OFF: a walk that never ends is joined until it fails (each request has the transport's own time limit). TESTS: `PlexLongWalkR471Tests` (three lookups after a 400 s walk cost one walk, and a fourth 301 s after it arrived walks again; a lookup during a 400 s walk joins it): before the change 3 failures (3 walks instead of 1; 4 instead of 2; 2 instead of 1), after it all pass, and the 76 other Plex tests and the end-to-end test pass. | (a) as built: count from arrival, always join a running walk; (b) count from the start but join a running walk whatever its age; (c) count from the start (before), with a longer lifetime for large libraries |
| R471 (71) | **Plex path review, finding B: looking up one file does not wait for a new walk of the whole library (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 3; work that repeated inside a time-limited step; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS, NOT MEASURED on a device).** FOUND: `PlexClient.info` and the file-address lookup (`partURL`, used by the first read of a file and by `playbackAccess`) waited for `currentTree()`; once the listing had expired (300 s, R305) that meant a full walk of the library (every section page and one request per show) inside steps with a time limit: the detail page's 8 s track probe (`MediaTrackProbe`, `Deadline.run`; on a large library the probe ran out and the rows kept "Not checked yet") and the start of a play. CHANGED: `lookupTree(for:)`: when the listing has expired or a walk is running, and the last complete listing (`heldTree`, R380) has the file, that listing answers at once and a new walk starts in the background (joined, not doubled, if one runs). A file the last listing lacks still waits for the new walk (it may be new on the server); folder lists (`list`, the scan) still wait. TRADE-OFF: within one walk's time after expiry, a file whose address changed on the server is first asked at its old address (a 404 forgets it, R471 (59)). TESTS: `PlexLookupDuringWalkR471Tests` (a lookup of a file the expired listing has answers while the new walk is held, and the walk is started; a folder list and a file not in the listing still wait). Run on the code before the change in a separate worktree: the first test failed ("answered while the walk was held: false"), the two "still waits" tests passed (they describe behaviour kept); after the change all pass, with the 78 other Plex tests and the end-to-end test. | (a) as built: the last listing answers one-file lookups while a new walk runs; (b) a longer time limit for the probe and the start instead; (c) look the file up with one request to the server (would need the item's ratingKey kept in the library; which request would serve it is not checked) |
| R471 (73) | **Plex path review, finding E: in Change Artwork, a picture still being fetched is not set after Automatic (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 3; state undone by a late answer; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** FOUND: `ArtworkGalleryView.choose` and `chooseWebAddress` set the chosen picture as an edit when the fetch (a download, or the server's picture for a Plex source) ended; Automatic is not disabled while a fetch runs, so Automatic pressed during the fetch removed the choice and then the fetch put the picture back. CHANGED: `choiceGeneration`: each choice and Automatic take a new number; a fetch sets its picture only when no newer choice or Automatic was made while it waited (the fetched file stays in the picture folder, unused, as a refused choice did before). TEST: `ScreenChecksTests.testAnArtworkChoiceThatArrivesAfterAutomaticIsNotSet` (structure, from the source); failed before the change (4 failures), passes after. | (a) as built: the newest choice or Automatic wins; (b) disable Automatic while a fetch runs; (c) let the fetch finish and then apply Automatic after it |
| R471 (74) | **Plex path review, finding F: a scan asked for during a scan is not lost (owner 25 Nov 2026 round 8, Part B item 3; a shared scan that did not cover its caller; wording ours; PROVISIONAL, NOT COMPILED for tvOS).** FOUND: `AppServices.scanAll` called while a scan ran only waited for that scan, which had read the list of sources when it started, so a source added during a scan (`sourceAdded`) was not scanned until the next scan. `rescanAfterSortChange` called it twice to cover a changed setting; the second call could find the finished scan's handle (cleared only after the first caller resumed) and return without scanning (depends on the order waiting callers resume; NOT VERIFIED which order the runtime uses). CHANGED: a call during a scan sets `scanAgain` and waits for the same scan, which goes round once more (sources, the online sort pass, Top Shelf, ratings) for every request made while it ran; the scan clears its own handle right after its last check, with no wait between, so a later call always starts a new scan. `rescanAfterSortChange` calls `scanAll` once. TEST: `ScreenChecksTests.testAScanAskedForDuringAScanIsNotLost` (structure, from the source); failed before the change (6 failures), passes after, with the other screen checks (175 passed, 12 skipped). | (a) as built: one more round per request made during a scan; (b) a new scan queued after the running one (same effect, two tasks); (c) scan only the added source when one is added during a scan |
